**Job:** [ROUTINE.md](ROUTINE.md), one iteration.

All **19** skills carry a `SKILL.md` with `name` + `description`. Broken internal `.md` links: **0** across a whole-repo crawl (Python resolver; re-run after edits, still 0). Freshness: AEO/GEO playbook `Last reviewed 2026-08-23` (0 days), `integrations/README.md` `2026-07-23` (31 days) — both inside 90. **Counts reconcile end to end at 72/19:** README badge + tagline + section headers, `AGENTS_INDEX.md` (header line + **72** table rows summing 7+7+7+7+5+5+6+7+1+2+5+2+1+1+1+4+4), `llms.txt`, `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the CATALYST discipline table (re-summed to 72), and the **live GitHub About** (`gh repo view` — "72 specialist agents and 19 Claude Code skills"). All 72 dual-located pairs diff-identical. No P0. Backlog: P1 high-leverage item blocked on maintainer decisions; the P1 distribution block is entirely traction-gated (repo at 5★, its own header says none is an unblocked pull); the legal-validation PROPOSAL needs a human — so the top genuinely-unblocked item was the P2 skill-curation affiliate question (line 105). Freshness targets (playbook + `seo-ai-search-optimizer`) were both reviewed *today*, so re-running them would have been padding.

**Shipped (backlog line 105 — decided *section*, not new persona).** Grep-verified the gap first, exactly as filed: `affiliate` appeared repo-wide only as sub-topics — newsletter *monetization* (`email-newsletter-growth-strategist`), a creator *comp model* (`social-influencer-partnerships`), *disclosure* (`ops-legal-compliance`), SERP competition + credibility (`seo-content-optimizer`), a UTM medium (`analytics-marketing-ops-architect`) — and `referral program` resolved only to *subscriber* (`email-newsletter-growth-strategist`) and *community* (`social-community-builder`) referral. **No agent ran a program paying third parties to refer customers.** Owner decided: `partner-ecosystem-marketer` (partnerships), whose whole thesis is marketing *with and through other companies' distribution* — the natural home, and its counterparty test (Rule 1) already routes an *individual* creator-as-affiliate to Influencer Partnerships while keeping the *program*. Added, both dual copies:
- **Core Mission bullet** — the affiliate/partner-referral program as an owned acquisition channel, paid on a qualified settled action.
- **Critical Rule 11** — four non-negotiables: commission model matched to unit economics with refund/chargeback/early-churn **clawback** in the terms; "cookie duration" = this channel's **attribution window** under the single-partner-per-deal rule (Rule 4), reconciled with the Attribution Analyst not run as a parallel last-click; **pay only on qualified settled action** behind identity verification, an activation gate (not signup), per-period caps and manual outlier review (fraud = self-referrals, referral rings, code leakage to coupon/deal sites); FTC material-connection **disclosure made structural** and routed to `ops-legal-compliance`. Plus two seams: the **customer** referral program is a different motion left to a maintainer call, and `email-newsletter-growth-strategist` runs affiliate the *inbound* direction (a newsletter earning commission) — same word, opposite cash flow.
- **Deliverable** — *Affiliate & Partner-Referral Program Design* (commission model + clawback, attribution window, recruitment/qualification, partner enablement kit, fraud/payout controls, PRM tooling decision, structural disclosure).
- **Success Metric** — affiliate *channel quality* not headcount: paid only on qualified settled actions, clawback/fraud rate trending down, reported inside partner-sourced ARR under the same sourced-or-influenced field.

**Discoverability.** No count/name change (still 72/19), so no full sweep. One capability-naming touch: the CATALYST Partnerships discipline row now reads "…cloud-marketplace GTM, **affiliate and partner-referral programs**" so the new capability is searchable. The agent's frontmatter `description` already abstractly covers this ("markets with and through other companies") and rewriting it would cascade to `AGENTS_INDEX.md` for no real gain — left as is.

**Sourcing.** Ideas-only. Both named sources license-verified MIT via `gh api .../license` (`coreyhaines31/marketingskills` → `skills/referrals/`; `teachskillofskills-ai/DigitalMarketingPro-techshu` → `skills/growth-engineering/affiliate-marketing.md`), read in full via the GitHub API. **No dollar figures or commission rates imported** — the second source explicitly flags its own numbers as unstamped priors, so everything numeric was written structurally (no percentages, no payout amounts). The framing, seams, and controls are the repo's.

**Deferred.** The customer-referral-program owner question (filed as a new open backlog item under the closed one) — a maintainer call between `growth-customer-marketing-lead` and `pmm-customer-advocacy`, not a scout call.

**Verify:** lint **2/2** on the two touched agent copies; the two copies diff-identical (`cp` + `diff -q`); referenced slugs resolve (`ops-legal-compliance`, `growth-customer-marketing-lead`, `pmm-customer-advocacy`, `email-newsletter-growth-strategist`); broken internal `.md` links **0** post-edit; counts unchanged at 72/19 including the live GitHub About. `CHANGELOG [Unreleased] › Changed` bullet added; backlog line 105 marked done, follow-up item opened.

### 2026-08-23 — Skill Scout: **ADD** — the media no auction sells finally has a buyer (automated)

**Scope:** scout survey, focus discipline **paid media / social** by rotation — last the focus on 2026-08-16, the oldest of the four (PMM/sales/GTM 08-17, content/SEO 08-18, organic social 08-19, email/analytics/ops 08-21). 12 sources evaluated — **1 adopted-new (+3 co-credit, +1 primary standards citation)**, 1 watch, 6 dismissed (1 of those declined on licensing). Full rows in [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**This run is an ADD, and deliberately so.** The 08-19 scout run added (`social-podcast-strategist`) and the 08-21 run enhanced (`analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`). Under the alternation rule the roster grows this run rather than only being edited, so the survey opened by looking for a missing role instead of a better version of an existing one.

**Shipped:** a new agent — [`paid-media/paid-media-sponsorship-syndication-buyer.md`](../paid-media/paid-media-sponsorship-syndication-buyer.md) (**Sponsorship & Syndication Buyer**), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/paid-media-ops/agents/`. Roster **71 → 72 agents**, Paid Media **6 → 7**. Skills unchanged at **19**, disciplines unchanged at **17** — the role sits inside the existing `paid-media-ops` skill, so no new directory was created.

**The gap, grep-verified before writing.** `content syndication`, `insertion order`, `pay-per-lead`, `vanity URL`, `make-good`, `G2 category` and `review generation` returned **zero** repo-wide. `media kit` resolved only to `comms-pr-strategist`'s press kit — the *press* artifact, not the buy-side one. `sponsorship` resolved to exactly two owners, both of them the wrong side of the transaction or the wrong medium: `email-newsletter-growth-strategist`, which sells sponsorships in *our* newsletter (down to CPM-versus-flat-fee pricing), and `events-field-marketing-strategist`, which buys conference sponsorship — a room, not a media slot. So the repo could sell a newsletter placement and buy a booth, and had no owner for buying a placement in someone else's newsletter, podcast, community or publication, for buying the paid units on G2/Capterra, or for buying leads per lead from a syndication network. This is the repo's recurring *dead-ending handoff* pattern seen from the other end: several agents consume this channel's outputs and none produces them.

**Demand evidence, checked before writing.** Four independent MIT-licensed sources, all licences verified via the GitHub license API this run: [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills) names **paid review listings** and **sponsorships** as two of five B2B paid channel families and flags the review listings as "often skipped" despite high intent; [LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills) ships content syndication as a first-class demand-gen skill; [maryakul/influencer-profile-research](https://github.com/maryakul/influencer-profile-research) is an entire skill about verifying a sponsorship seller's claimed reach; [guerrilla2799/event-and-scale-os](https://github.com/guerrilla2799/event-and-scale-os) ships sponsorship negotiation as its own discipline. Two very large collections ([alirezarezvani](https://github.com/alirezarezvani/claude-skills), [davila7](https://github.com/davila7/claude-code-templates)) mention content syndication exactly once each — as a ToFu tactic in a funnel table with no owner — which is the pattern itself: the channel gets listed constantly and owned almost never.

**What is ours.** (a) The organising asymmetry: **in an auction the platform counts the impressions and you argue about the price; in a direct buy the seller counts everything and the argument is about the truth** — every figure on a media kit was produced by the party being paid, and nobody audits it. (b) The **buy-side mirror of a rule this repo already holds**: `email-newsletter-growth-strategist` tells its operator not to quote a sponsor an open-inflated reach; nothing told the buyer what to do with the number they are quoted. Rule 1 routes the definition question to the two places the repo already documents the instruments — machine-contaminated email engagement in `email-deliverability-specialist` Rule 9, download-is-a-file-request in `social-podcast-strategist` Rule 4 — rather than re-deriving either. (c) **Repeat sponsors are the only external audit available**, because a buyer on their third insertion has conversion data you do not, and the same read doubles as brand safety. (d) The **placement-versus-endorsement line**: you buy a slot in someone's distribution; the moment the deal requires the host to speak in their own voice it becomes a material connection and belongs to `social-influencer-partnerships` under the § 255.5 discipline `social-podcast-strategist` states — a rate card must not be allowed to convert one into the other quietly. (e) **There is no pixel, so the measurement plan is a contract term** — four artifacts that must exist before the send, because nothing about a newsletter that already went out can be reconstructed. (f) **One insertion is not a test**, with two mechanisms (frequency, and a response window that outlives the flight — a podcast back-catalogue keeps returning traffic for months), routed to `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`'s feasibility discipline; the expensive outcome is not a wasted insertion but a channel closed for years on evidence that never existed. (g) The **deliverables register** and the renewal-amnesia rule. (h) A **paid review-listing** section holding the bright line that money buys placement and never sentiment, with anything touching a rating referred to `pmm-customer-advocacy` and `ops-legal-compliance`. (i) An explicit **boundary rule (Rule 10)** naming the thirteen agents that own everything on either side of the buy.

**The two facts that carry the most weight are contract facts, and both are quoted verbatim from a primary source read 2026-08-23** — the [AAAA/IAB Standard Terms and Conditions for Internet Advertising for Media Buys One Year or Less, v3.0](https://www.iab.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/06/IAB_4As-tsandcs-FINAL.pdf), downloaded and read in full. First: the standard's own preamble says it "may not fully cover sponsorships and other arrangements involving content association or integration, and/or special production" and "is not meant to cover … direct advertiser buys with publishers" — so the terms most digital insertion orders incorporate **exclude, by their own text, the deals a B2B SaaS company actually does**, and everything that matters has to be written in. Second, **§VI(c)**: where an IO contains CPA, CPL or CPC deliverables, "guaranteed delivery, even delivery, and makegoods are not available" — so the moment content syndication is bought on a cost-per-lead basis the delivery guarantee is gone, and its cheap-looking CPL is priced partly by the protection it removes. Neither appears in any surveyed marketing skill. **No claim is made about how widely those terms are adopted**, only about what they say.

**No fabricated metrics.** No CPM, CPL, conversion-rate, lead-acceptance, open-rate or channel benchmark figure appears anywhere in the agent — which is also why its success metrics are coverage and discipline measures (qualification coverage, contract completeness, measurement readiness at launch, delivery reconciliation, single-insertion verdicts targeted at zero) rather than targets. The vendor-report figures the LeadMagic source carries (syndication CPL ranges, lead-to-SQO rates, a 47.7-hour consumption gap) were **deliberately not carried**.

**Discoverability (counts changed, so the full sweep ran).** `AGENTS_INDEX.md` (header count, Paid Media 6 → 7, new row, regeneration date), README (tagline, install line, feature bullet, team table row, discipline count, skills-trigger row with `newsletter sponsorship` / `content syndication`, and **both badges**), `llms.txt` (summary, agent-index line, Paid Media Ops skill line, CATALYST line), `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the suite router (prose counts, discipline table, quick-reference table and TOTAL), the CATALYST orchestrator `SKILL.md`, both copies of `catalyst-strategy.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `strategy/QUICKSTART.md`, `strategy/EXECUTIVE-BRIEF.md`, `integrations/README.md`, `loops/README.md`, the owning skill's `description:` frontmatter (which gained the phrases a marketer would actually type — `newsletter sponsorship`, `sponsor a newsletter`, `content syndication`, `pay-per-lead`, `media kit`, `rate card`, `insertion order`, `direct buy`, `make-good`, `G2 paid listing`, `Capterra ads`, `review site advertising`), the skill's routing block (five new prompts), and the **live GitHub About description** (`gh repo edit`, 345/350 chars, now naming "newsletter sponsorships & content syndication"). Grep for `71` outside `maintenance/`, `CHANGELOG.md` and the AEO/GEO playbook's `0.66–0.71` correlation returns nothing.

**Also fixed (count correctness, not a second change).** Two tallies were already stale before this run and would have been wrong either way: the suite router's quick-reference table still read `Social Media Operations | 6` and `TOTAL | 70`, missed by the 2026-08-19 podcast add, and the CATALYST functional-category lists in both copies of `catalyst-strategy.md` still summed to 70. All three now reconcile to **72**, with the two genuinely new roles (Podcast & Audio Strategist, Sponsorship & Syndication Buyer) added to their functional categories.

**Verified:** lint **72/72**; the two copies of the new agent are **diff-identical**, and every one of the 72 dual-located pairs is byte-identical except the one known, intended relative-link difference in `catalyst-strategy.md`; canonical and plugin agent counts both **72**; both manifests parse as JSON; **532 internal links checked, 0 broken**; the new external URL (IAB v3.0 PDF) was downloaded and read this run; the live GitHub About description re-read after the edit and confirmed at 72.

**Filed for future runs:** the reciprocal seam — `events-field-marketing-strategist` and `social-influencer-partnerships` state their boundaries from their own side but do not yet name this agent, the same one-sided-seam pattern the 2026-08-16 enforcement-register item raised and the 08-22 run closed for QA; `guerrilla2799/event-and-scale-os`'s remaining 23 event skills as an events-run watch; and `teachskillofskills-ai/DigitalMarketingPro-techshu`'s `affiliate-marketing` skill against the repo's genuinely unowned affiliate/referral-program question (`affiliate` currently grazes five agents and is owned by none).

### 2026-08-23 — Maintenance: **AEO/GEO freshness pass — a 2026 critical survey bounds the GEO effect sizes to already-retrieved content, and the playbook + AI-search agent now say so** (automated)

**Job:** [ROUTINE.md](ROUTINE.md), one iteration.

**Health check (all clean, no P0).** Broken internal `.md` links: **0** across a whole-repo crawl (205 markdown files, 486 internal `.md` links, Python resolver; re-run after edits, still 0). Manifests: `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` and `plugins/saas-marketing/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` both parse with required fields (`jq`). All **19** skills carry a `SKILL.md` with `name` + `description`. Freshness (pre-run): AEO/GEO playbook `Last reviewed 2026-08-13` (10 days), `integrations/README.md` `2026-07-23` (31 days) — both inside 90 days. Counts reconcile end to end and unchanged: **71** specialist agents (`find` over the discipline dirs, files with `name:` frontmatter; `strategy/` CATALYST docs and the four `loops/` + `integrations/README.md` non-agent files excluded) = README badges + tagline = `AGENTS_INDEX.md` = `llms.txt` = `CITATION.cff` = both manifests = the **live GitHub About** (`gh repo view` = "71 specialist marketing agents and 19 installable Claude Code skills"). No P0.

**Backlog state → freshness pass (routine step 3).** P0: none open. Every P1 is blocked or done — native subagents [#1](https://github.com/shalintripathi/saas-marketing-agents/issues/1) on two in-thread decisions; all four awesome-list/marketplace submissions traction/process-gated at ~5 stars (re-verified 2026-08-18); the `ops-legal-compliance` proposal on human legal validation. The genuinely-unblocked backlog remainder is scout-read/maintainer-scope items, not clean ships: **re-checked and dismissed two this run** — line 84 (`portermetricsample/marketing-skills` license still `null` via GitHub API 2026-08-20 → stays ideas-only/unread, re-dating alone would be padding) and line 91 (`modest-curator478/claude-skills`, MIT: its `free-trial-designer` is **already substantially owned** by `growth-plg-activation-strategist`'s trial-architecture section + Trial Model Decision Memo — grep-confirmed — so not a gap; `martech-contract-auditor` + the `leadership/` tree feed the open "grade the marketing function" maintainer scope-question, line 85, not a clean ship). So ran the designated fallback: a freshness pass on the AEO/GEO playbook + `seo-ai-search-optimizer`.

**Shipped.** A verified 2026 development the 2026-08-13 review predates: **"Optimizing Visibility in Generative Engines: A Critical Survey of GEO 2023–2026"** ([Martinez, arXiv:2607.14035](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14035), submitted 2026-07-15), a critical survey of **45 studies**. Its findings are the strongest independent caveat available on the KDD-2024 effect sizes both files rely on (and that the agent turns into a scoring rubric): *"no reviewed technique shows a stable, longitudinal, cross-platform causal effect on organic discoverability"*; the one replicating effect is bounded — only **already-retrieved content can causally alter its own citation or use**; commercial GEO tools show *"low source overlap, substantial run-to-run variability, and persistent fidelity gaps."* This maps exactly onto the playbook's existing discovery-vs-citation split. Added: a flagged **"Reality check (independent 2026 survey)"** callout after the §2 effect-size table (the hinge between "these levers move citations of retrieved content" and the correlational off-page discovery signals below it); the survey in **Sources**; `Last reviewed` → **2026-08-23**. In both dual-located copies of `seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`, the existing directional-caveat paragraph gains the same bound — the rubric scores whether a *retrieved* passage earns the citation, does not promise retrieval, and a score that climbs while real citation share doesn't is the documented run-to-run variability, not a win to defend.

**What's verified vs. asserted.** Verified against the **primary source** (arXiv abstract, read 2026-08-23); all three quoted phrases verbatim. **No effect sizes changed** — none was contradicted, only bounded (the +40%/+33%/… table stands as directional levers *within* an answer). No fabricated figures; the survey is an independent secondary source added alongside, not replacing, the KDD-2024 primary.

**Verify.** Lint **2/2** on both agent copies (`bash scripts/lint-agents.sh`); the two copies **diff-identical**; **0 broken internal links** across 486 links after edits; counts unchanged at **71 / 19** everywhere including the live GitHub About → no count/name/capability-in-index change, so no discoverability sweep owed (frontmatter descriptions untouched and still accurate). `CHANGELOG.md` `[Unreleased] → Changed` gains the user-facing bullet.

**Deferred.** The maintainer-scope items stay open: line 85 (is a "grade the marketing function" persona in scope), line 96's open half (enforcement-register ownership + combined pre-launch gate), line 101 (synthetic-voice disclosure, belongs on the blocked `ops-legal-compliance` EU-AI-Act proposal), line 103 (podcast Rule 9 named method — only act with a source better than an invented rubric). The P2 architecture items (golden examples, GitHub Pages catalog, MCP recipes, eval harness, source de-dup) exceed one-small-change.

### 2026-08-22 — Maintenance: **the webinar→podcast seam gets named on both sides — a recorded webinar's audio afterlife is events' to plan but the podcast strategist's feed to run** (automated)

**Job:** [ROUTINE.md](ROUTINE.md), one iteration.

**Health check (all clean, no P0).** Broken internal `.md` links: **0** across a whole-repo crawl (relative-path resolver over every tracked `*.md`; re-run after edits, still 0). Manifests: `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` and `plugins/saas-marketing/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` both parse with required fields (`jq`). All **19** skills carry a `SKILL.md` with `name` + `description`. Freshness: AEO/GEO playbook `Last reviewed 2026-08-13` (9 days), `integrations/README.md` `2026-07-23` (30 days) — both inside 90 days. Counts reconcile end to end and unchanged: **71** specialist agents (`find` over the 17 discipline dirs, `strategy/` = CATALYST content not agents, README excluded) = README badges + tagline = `AGENTS_INDEX.md` = `llms.txt` = `CITATION.cff` = both manifests = the **live GitHub About** (`gh repo view` = "71 specialist marketing agents and 19 installable Claude Code skills"). No P0.

**Shipped (top unblocked item; all P1 blocked — native-subagents on issue #1 decisions, every distribution item on star/usage gates, the marketplace form + legal-compliance proposal needing human action).** Backlog line 102: whether the webinar engine (`events-field-marketing-strategist`) and the podcast agent (`social-podcast-strategist`, shipped 2026-08-19) share a seam worth naming — with the standing instruction to grep first and prefer no change to a redundant one. Grep decided it: the seam was **asymmetric, not redundant.** The podcast agent's Rule 10 named only the **conference-room** direction ("a live episode recorded at a conference is their buy and your production"); the **webinar-recording-republished-as-a-podcast-episode** direction was named by neither agent — the events agent's Rule 10 seam list (6 downstream owners: Campaign Coordinator, Presentation Designer, Content Video Script Writer, Ad Creative Producer, Outbound Strategist, Customer Advocacy Manager) omitted the podcast strategist entirely, and its "on-demand repurposing plan" / Rule 8 "on-demand afterlife" never routed an audio episode to a podcast feed, silently implying events owns the audio afterlife end to end. ### 2026-08-21 — Skill Scout: **ENHANCE** — the sample-size calculation is allowed to say "do not run this test" (automated)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration. **This run ENHANCED.** The 08-19 run added (`social-podcast-strategist`), so the alternation rule put an enhancement first this run. Discipline by rotation: **email / analytics / marketing-ops**, last the focus on 2026-08-13 and the oldest of the four (content/SEO 08-14 and 08-18, paid/social 08-16, PMM/sales/GTM 08-17, organic social 08-19). 12 sources evaluated — 1 enhance (+3 co-credit), 2 watch, the rest dismissed. Full rows in [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**Shipped:** both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md`](../analytics/analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer.md) gain **Critical Rule 10**, a **"Before You Design the Test: Can This Funnel Power It?"** section, a **Test Feasibility Record** deliverable, and a **Feasibility Discipline** success metric. No agent or skill count changed (71 / 19), so no count sweep was owed.

**The gap.** Rule 2 already required a sample-size calculation before launch, and the 2026-08-10 trust layer (`Trust the Split Before the Winner`) already governed whether a *finished* test is readable — SRM, optional stopping, guardrails, novelty. Neither answered the question that comes before both: **can this funnel produce the sample at all?** In practice the calculation is run to *schedule* a test already chosen, so it answers "how long will this take" and never "should this exist." Grep-verified before writing: `expected loss`, `bandit`, `Thompson`, `false discovery`, and `multiple comparison` returned **zero** hits repo-wide, and `underpowered` resolved only to the paid-creative power rule and this agent's own `extend` verdict — nowhere did the repo say what to do when a test can never be powered.

**The thesis, and what is ours.** The required sample scales with the **inverse square** of the effect, so at B2B volumes the arithmetic frequently returns a requirement the site cannot supply — a 3% page tested for a 20% relative lift needs ≈13,900 per arm, ≈9 months at 3,000 visits/month (recomputed from the standard formula at Z = 1.96 / 0.84; illustrative inputs, not asserted as typical). The original argument is what nine months *means*: **the ceiling on test duration is comparability, not patience.** `analytics-performance-analyst` keeps a series-break register precisely because instruments and businesses change on their own schedule, so every surface has a **comparability horizon** — the longest window it has historically gone unchanged — and a test whose required duration exceeds it is not slow, it is unreadable. That reframes the market's flat "what if it doesn't fit" menus into a gate with a derived ceiling. Five reroutes are ranked around the inverse-square property (bigger swing · move upstream · pool near-identical surfaces · move it to the list · randomize the account), a sixth outcome — **decide without a test, logged as a decision and not a result** — is made legitimate, and three market-listed "options" are refused: lowering α from 95% to 90% (≈21% off the sample, roughly double the false-winner rate — a subsidy, not a trade), adding arms, and reaching for Bayes because N is small.

**The inversion.** Two independent sources recommend Bayesian analysis *specifically when traffic is low*. **A posterior is not more data** — at small N it is substantially shaped by the prior, so P(best) = 92% over forty conversions is partly a claim about the prior. What Bayes genuinely adds is **expected loss**, which prices being wrong in the metric's own units, and the terminal verdict of **practical equivalence** (every arm's expected loss below a tolerance declared in advance, in business terms) — a way to *retire* a question rather than re-open it quarterly. Two obligations attach: declare the prior before the data, and never report P(best) without expected loss beside it. Bandits get two objections: they optimize the metric visible *now* while the B2B metric that decides (SQL, pipeline) lands weeks later — Rule 4's failure mode with a throttle on it — and adaptive splits disable the flat-split SRM check, which must then compare observed exposures against the allocator's *intended* allocation per period, or state SRM as unverifiable.

**Attribution / licensing:** ideas-only, written from scratch, no text reused. MIT-credited in-file to [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills) (`ab-testing` sample-size guide — the seven-option menu including the honest "don't test"), [OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills](https://github.com/OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills) (`ab-test-setup` — the only source putting a duration ceiling on the decision, though at a fixed eight weeks rather than a derived horizon), [sidchaudhary/gtm-skills](https://github.com/sidchaudhary/gtm-skills) (`references/bayesian-testing.md` — P(best), expected loss, practical equivalence as a terminal exit rule, and the intended-vs-flat allocation point), and [thatrebeccarae/claude-marketing](https://github.com/thatrebeccarae/claude-marketing) (`ab-testing-framework` — the low-traffic Bayesian claim, inverted here). All four licences verified via the GitHub license API 2026-08-21. **This closes the Bayesian slice of the standing 2026-08-18 `watch`** on the gtm-skills reference tree. No conversion rate, win rate, or lift figure asserted as typical; the worked figures are arithmetic from a stated formula and were verified by recomputation.

**Discoverability (no count change, capability change).** The new capability's natural phrases reach the owning skill's `description:` frontmatter (`sample size calculation`, `minimum detectable effect`, `test duration`, `not enough traffic to A/B test`, `Bayesian vs frequentist`, `sample ratio mismatch`, `experiment feasibility`), the skill's routing block (three new prompts a marketer would actually type), the skill's agent table row, the README trigger row for Marketing Analytics, `llms.txt`, and the agent's own `description:` — mirrored into the `AGENTS_INDEX.md` row so the two do not drift.

**Verified:** lint **2/2** on both copies; the two copies are **diff-identical** (canonical copied over the plugin copy); counts unchanged and re-confirmed at 71 agents / 19 skills; broken internal links **0** across a whole-repo crawl.

**Deferred:** `references/data-input-integrity.md` from the same source read in full and **dismissed as substantially covered** — the partial-period trap, timezone and identity alignment, the visible-denominator and absolute-floor rules, and dating a row to the sale all already live in `analytics-performance-analyst`'s comparability freeze and maturity sections; the residual (currency mixing, line-item-vs-order granularity, silent export truncation, placeholder values parsed as numbers) is e-commerce-shaped, and only the last two are plausibly ours. Filed as a P2 watch. `references/audit-findings-discipline.md` read and filed as a second P2 watch — an audit's **re-audit trigger** (an event, not a fixed interval) and a **severity × effort** grid are on-thesis for `ops-quality-assurance` and `seo-technical-auditor` but belong to a different discipline's run.

So — as on 2026-08-19 — pulled a self-directed, grep-verified *instrument-absent* gap on the repo's thinnest, most foundational SEO agent.

**Shipped:** both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-keyword-researcher.md`](../seo/seo-keyword-researcher.md) (56 → 72 lines; **first enhancement this agent has ever received**) gain **Critical Rule 9**, a **"The AI-Answer Query Landscape: Discover What Classic Volume Can't See"** section, an **AI-Answer Query Landscape Map** deliverable, and an **AI-query landscape coverage** success metric.

**The gap, grep-verified before writing.** The Keyword Researcher is the designated owner of "the entire addressable search landscape," yet it mapped only the classic typed-search landscape — **zero** mentions of AI search, AEO, prompts, conversational queries, query fan-out, or entity sets. Two of the repo's signature patterns at once: (1) *control named, instrument absent* — `seo-ai-search-optimizer` scores citability/recognition **against a query set it assumes already exists** (the share-of-voice heatmap's tracked-query rows, the brand-question set, the "5–10 core topics" are all consumed, never discovered); (2) a *dead-ending handoff* — `seo-programmatic-strategist` Rule 10 already declares "Keyword Researcher hands you the *pattern* and the entity set," a reference the Keyword Researcher's file never acknowledged.

**The correction, and the seam.** The agent gets the *discovery* half: reframe head/commercial terms into the natural-language questions people ask assistants; build the **follow-up tree** (the buyer's next question is the valuable unit); account for Google's **query fan-out** (one prompt explodes into many sub-queries, each answered from a different passage — per the AEO/GEO Playbook, already cited to Google); mine real phrasing (PAA, community threads, sales-call transcripts, support tickets) over invention; and cluster by *the answer an engine would synthesize*, not by lexical overlap. The load-bearing rule is **zero volume is not zero demand** — a conversational query classic tools score at zero may be the exact question a high-intent account asks the week before it shortlists, so it is flagged *unmeasured — no volume denominator*, never *low-priority* (Rule 1's value-over-volume at its limit; *unknown never rounds to zero*). The seam is drawn explicitly so nothing duplicates the AEO optimizer: **discovery here; citability scoring, the recognition test, the heatmap, and citation claims stay with `seo-ai-search-optimizer`; the template-able question-shape-plus-entity-set goes to `seo-programmatic-strategist`** (closing that dead-ending handoff from the receiving side too).

**Sourcing.** Self-directed gap, no external source (same class as the 2026-08-19 link-building and 2026-07-31 QA ships); the query-fan-out mechanism references the repo's own AEO/GEO Playbook. **No search-volume, traffic, or demand figure asserted** — the new metric reports questions-observed-in-a-real-source vs. questions-inferred, never a percentage. The agent's pre-existing aspirational success-metric ranges (e.g. "40-60% traffic increase") were **left untouched this run** to keep scope to one change; noted as a future anti-fabrication cleanup candidate.

**Discoverability (no count change — naming surfaces only, matching the 2026-08-18 precedent).** A capability genuinely expanded, so the agent's frontmatter `description:` now names it ("typed search and AI-answer-engine queries"), with the matching `AGENTS_INDEX.md` row and the `seo-growth` skill's agent-table row updated to the same. No badge/manifest/`CITATION.cff`/About sweep owed (roster unchanged). Old description string grep-confirmed gone from all three surfaces.

**Verified:** lint **2/2** (both copies); the two copies are **diff-identical**; both new agent-slug references (`seo-ai-search-optimizer`, `seo-programmatic-strategist`) resolve to real files; broken internal `.md` links **0**; counts still **71 / 19** everywhere including the live GitHub About. `CHANGELOG` `## [Unreleased] → ### Changed` bullet added (user-facing).

So pulled a self-directed, grep-verified *instrument-absent* gap on a first-pass agent — the repo's most-repeated ship type (e.g. the 2026-07-31 QA discrimination control, which likewise had no external source).

**Shipped:** both dual-located copies of [`seo/seo-link-building-strategist.md`](../seo/seo-link-building-strategist.md) (59 → 81 lines; **first enhancement this agent has ever received**) gain **Critical Rule 9**, a **"The Links You Didn't Earn: The Inbound Audit and the Disavow of Last Resort"** section, an **Inbound Profile Audit & Disavow Decision Record** deliverable, and a **Disavow discipline** success metric — plus a one-line reciprocal seam in both copies of [`seo/seo-technical-auditor.md`](../seo/seo-technical-auditor.md). The agent earned links in eight rules and never addressed the links pointing *at* the site. **Gap grep-verified before writing:** `disavow`, `negative seo`, `backlink audit`, `toxic link`, `link audit`, `link detox`, `inbound link` = **0** repo-wide; the only `unnatural link` (2) / `reconsideration` (4) hits were all in `seo-technical-auditor`'s manual-action branch, which reads the binary and routes "to remediation plus a reconsideration request" — to an owner that did not exist until now (the recurring *dead-ending handoff* pattern). The correction is a **posture, not a tool**: SpamBrain **nullifies** spammy links algorithmically (Dec 2022 link spam update — "the link credit… is lost… this is not a manual action"), so the standing action on an ugly inbound link is *do nothing and record why*, and the "toxic link score" third-party tools sell is cargo-cult work. Disavow is gated on the single binary — an *"Unnatural links to your site"* manual action — after which remediation follows Google's stated order (remove first → disavow only the remainder → reconsideration narrative), under the tool's own "advanced feature… can potentially harm your site's performance" warning. Value-add over a generic "toxic links" skill: the last-resort gating with the SpamBrain mechanism, the one-binary trigger, the remove-first order, the reconsideration-as-narrative deliverable, the "when in doubt a link stays" guardrail, and the reciprocal seam naming.

**Sources (primary, read 2026-08-19, quoted-and-cited, not from memory):** [December 2022 link spam update](https://developers.google.com/search/blog/2022/12/december-22-link-spam-update), [Disavow links — Search Console Help](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/2648487), [Manual actions report](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/9044175). No external open-source skill used; **no ranking, recovery-time, or link-count figure asserted.**

**Verified after editing:** lint **4/4** (both changed agents, both copies) via `scripts/lint-agents.sh`; both dual copies **diff-identical**; broken internal `.md` links **0/461**; counts still **71 / 19** everywhere including the live GitHub About (roster unchanged → no discoverability sweep owed). `CHANGELOG` `## [Unreleased] → ### Changed` bullet added (user-facing). No backlog item marked done — this was a self-directed gap, not a listed item.

**Deferred:** the podcast Rule 9 grading method (needs a real external method, not an invented rubric); the webinar/podcast seam (judged redundant per its own item); all P1 (blocked) and P2-"later" (issue-territory) items.

### 2026-08-19 — Skill Scout: **ADD** — the largest untouched channel in the repo finally has an owner (automated)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration. **This run ADDED.** The 08-18 run enhanced, so the alternation rule put an add first this run; the discipline rotation put **organic social** in focus, last a primary focus on 2026-08-07.

**Shipped:** a new agent — [`social/social-podcast-strategist.md`](../social/social-podcast-strategist.md), **Podcast & Audio Strategist** (🎙️), dual-located to [`plugins/saas-marketing/skills/social-media-ops/agents/`](../plugins/saas-marketing/skills/social-media-ops/agents/social-podcast-strategist.md). Roster **70 → 71**; Social Media **6 → 7**; skills unchanged at **19**; no new discipline directory (audio sits with the other channel specialists, next to its closest sibling `social-youtube-producer`, rather than earning a directory of its own for one agent).

**Gap proof, before a word was written.** `audiogram`, `RSS`, `Apple Podcasts`, `Spotify`, `podcast tour`, `podcast guesting`, `guest booking` and `host-read` all returned **zero** hits repo-wide. `podcast` resolved to five passing mentions across five files (a repurposing bullet, a distribution aside, a brand-context prompt), and the single substantive `show notes` line sat in `social-youtube-producer`'s deliverables — *"repurpose into podcast distribution strategy with transcription and show notes"* — a handoff to an owner that did not exist. That is the repo's signature **dead-ending handoff**, the same pattern that preceded the E-E-A-T ship (08-14) and the instrumentation-contract ship (08-18).

**Why this role and not another.** Three tests, all met. *No existing agent owns it*: the YouTube Producer owns a video channel's watch-time mechanics, `comms-pr-strategist` owns journalists and editorial outlets, `social-influencer-partnerships` owns compensated creator relationships — none of the three answers "should we have a show" or "who should be on it." *Durable, not a fad*: B2B audio has been a stable channel for a decade and the guest tour is an established founder-led motion. *Real demand evidence*: three independent MIT-licensed podcast skills published in the last ~10 months, and the market's own framing corroborates the B2B angle — a 2026 practitioner guide describes the "guest-to-pipeline motion" and argues B2B teams get more from podcasting when it is treated as demand generation than as a brand side-project.

**The thesis, and what it inverts.** The market's fullest podcast skill ([OpenClaudia](https://github.com/OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills)) is built for a **creator media business** — sponsorship rates at download tiers, host-read CPMs, first-7-day downloads driving chart position. For B2B SaaS that scoreboard is not merely irrelevant, it is *inverting*: a show optimizing for chart position books guests for reach, and the guest booking is the one decision that determines whether a B2B show returns anything. So the agent starts at two questions nobody in the market owns: **should this company run a show at all** (a four-leg readiness test — named host with a protected calendar block, named producer, 8–12 episodes banked pre-launch, a cadence holdable in the team's *worst* month — with the guest tour as the honest answer when a leg is missing, because an abandoned feed stays public as evidence the company quits things), and **who should be on it** (the guest list sourced from `abm-account-based-strategist`'s target list, under a bright line that the invitation is not a sales sequence and the recording is not a discovery call).

**The other seven rules,** in brief: a download is a server-log file request, not a person (**IAB Tech Lab Podcast Measurement Technical Guidelines v2.2, May 2024**, which measures from server logs and treats downloads/audience/ad-delivery as separate categories — read 2026-08-19); the transcript is the compounding asset and must be proofread, because ASR mangles exactly the tokens that matter and the error becomes the canonical text an answer engine lifts; record video by default but the YouTube channel is not yours; rights, consent and takedown settled in writing before the record button, with customer guests routed through `pmm-customer-advocacy`'s expiring consent record; material connections disclosed **in the spoken audio** under **16 CFR § 255.5** (verified against Cornell LII 2026-08-19 — distinct from the Part 465 review rule this repo already cites); the guest tour qualified on observable audience evidence rather than self-reported download claims, with genuine press outlets routed to `comms-pr-strategist`; and Rule 10 drawing explicit non-overlap against **nine** named agents, including the seam where a host relationship becomes *compensated* and moves to `social-influencer-partnerships`.

**Attribution / licensing:** ideas-only, written from scratch, no text reused. MIT-credited in-file and in the ledger to [OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills](https://github.com/OpenClaudia/openclaudia-skills) `podcast-marketing`, [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) `podcast-and-audiograms` (whose disciplined scope-and-connections reference shaped Rules 6 and 10, and which **closes the standing 2026-08-07 `watch`** on that repo), and [OneWave-AI/claude-skills](https://github.com/OneWave-AI/claude-skills) `podcast-content-suite` (episode-as-repurposing-hub, behind the Episode Asset Pack). All three licences verified via the GitHub license API this run. **No download, listener, conversion or pipeline benchmark asserted** — every success metric is a definition, not a number.

**Discoverability (counts changed, so the full sweep ran).** README (tagline, install line, feature bullet, **agents badge 70 → 71**, nav link, team-table Social Media row + count, skills-table row with `"podcast"` / `"show notes"` / `"podcast guesting"` triggers, and the disciplines-hold-all line), `AGENTS_INDEX.md` (new row + section count + header + regeneration date), `llms.txt` (counts ×3 plus the Social Media Ops line now naming the podcast capability), `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the suite router table, both CATALYST orchestrator tables **and the orchestrator's `description:` frontmatter** (which was doubly stale — `70 specialist agents across 16 marketing disciplines`, fixed to 71/17), `strategy/EXECUTIVE-BRIEF.md` (also corrected `12 functional categories` → `17 disciplines` on the roster line), `strategy/catalyst-strategy.md` both copies, `strategy/QUICKSTART.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `integrations/README.md`, `loops/README.md`, and the owning skill's `description:` frontmatter (now carrying `podcast`, `podcast strategy`, `podcast guesting`, `show notes`, `audiogram`, `episode transcript`) plus a seven-line routing block. **GitHub About description updated in the same run.** Grep-verified: the only remaining `70`s are percentages, the `40-70` scaling ranges in QUICKSTART, and dated historical entries in `CHANGELOG.md` / this log.

**Verified:** lint **2/2** on both copies; the two copies are **diff-identical**; **71** agent files across the 17 discipline directories against **71** rows in `AGENTS_INDEX.md`; both manifests parse; **0 broken internal links** across a whole-repo crawl (480 internal `.md` links checked).

**Deferred:** nothing new from this run's sources. Two pre-existing `watch` rows carried forward unchanged — `modest-curator478/claude-skills`' `revops/` + `leadership/` trees (for a future **ops** run) and `sidchaudhary/gtm-skills`' reference tree.

The top concrete, unblocked item was the 2026-08-17 scout's flagged paid-run enhancement: give `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` the **paid-ABM execution layer** that `abm-account-based-strategist`'s Rule 7 already hands here but nothing owned.

- Added **Critical Rule 11** + a new section, *"The Account List Is a Media Object: Building and Delivering the Paid ABM Buy"*, covering four mechanics: (1) **one matched audience/campaign per tier** — a blended list forfeits per-tier budget, frequency, and reporting and lets delivery concentrate off the strategic few; tiers under LinkedIn's 300-match floor route elsewhere rather than blend up to size; (2) **match rate is coverage, not setup** — Meta's hash-and-match means a work-email-only list matches a smaller share of itself; fixes are more identifiers per contact and preferring LinkedIn's company-to-company match for enumerated lists; (3) **air cover buys reach, not conversions** — an open-pipeline audience can't produce the ~50 events/7 days a conversion ad set needs to exit Meta's learning phase, so it runs on reach and is scored on penetration/progression; (4) a **self-rotating engagement exclusion** for frequency — engaged accounts graduate into an exclusion so prospecting budget keeps refreshing.
- Added a matching **Deliverable** ("Paid ABM Audience Construction & Delivery Plan") and a **Success Metric** (tier-segmented delivery + matched-share-as-coverage), consistent with the agent's existing pattern.

**Sourcing discipline.** Every platform claim quoted from and cited to primary docs read 2026-08-17: Meta [Customer List Custom Audiences](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/341425252616329) / [customer-info best practices](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/606443329504150) (match mechanics), [About the learning phase](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/112167992830700) (~50 events/7 days), [Frequency Controls for Reservation](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/285326585139636); LinkedIn [Retargeting with Matched Audiences](https://www.linkedin.com/help/lms/answer/a427551) and [campaign targeting Include/Exclude](https://www.linkedin.com/help/lms/answer/a420751), plus the ≥300-match floor already cited in the ABM agent. Ideas-only, MIT-credited to [coreyhaines31/marketingskills](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills); the source's unsourced go/no-go thresholds (list size, deal size, budget floor) deliberately **not** carried; the work-email match penalty flagged directional (Meta publishes no match rate by identifier type).

**Verified.** Both dual copies (`paid-media/` and `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/paid-media-ops/agents/`) edited to be diff-identical; `scripts/lint-agents.sh` passes **2/2**; broken-link crawl still 0; no count changed, so no discoverability ripple.

**Deferred.** Backlog line 94 marked done. The related maintainer decisions (line 92 combined pre-launch gate ownership; line 95 ABM-as-slash-commands architecture) remain open and are not scout calls.

### 2026-08-16 — the Google surface of the ad-policy discipline (automated maintenance-routine run)

**Job:** [ROUTINE.md](ROUTINE.md), one iteration — health check, then the top unblocked backlog item.

**Health check:** clean, no P0. `marketplace.json` and the one `plugin.json` parse with required fields intact; every `SKILL.md` (13) carries `name` + `description`; **0 broken internal `.md` links** repo-wide; the only two "Last reviewed" docs are inside the 90-day window (`guides/aeo-geo-playbook.md` 2026-08-13; `integrations/README.md` 2026-07-23). All P1 items remain blocked on maintainer decisions (persona subagents #1, `seo-programmatic` #2, `ops-legal-compliance` legal validation) or are gated/external distribution submissions not appropriate to auto-take unattended. Pulled the top **unblocked** actionable item, backlog line 91.

**Shipped:** `paid-media/paid-media-ppc-strategist` (both dual-located copies) — new **Critical Rule 11**, a **"The policy gate: running inside Google's rulebook"** section under Operating a Live Account, a **Search Ad Policy Pre-Flight** deliverable, and a **Policy pre-flight discipline** success metric.

**The gap, grep-verified before writing.** The 2026-08-16 Meta/LinkedIn ship deliberately left Google's surface to this agent, which had an operational spend-change gate and a learning-phase section but **no policy dimension at all**: `ad polic`, `disapprov`, `trademark`, `restricted categor`, and `account suspen` all returned **zero** on both copies of the PPC strategist. Same run, different agent, distinct surface — so a one-change-per-run follow-on, not a duplicate.

**Referenced, not restated.** The shipped Social Ads section owns the shared machinery — account-scale-before-ad-scale ordering, the four dispositions (pass / fix required / block / unreviewable), *approved never rounds to compliant*, *quote the clause or drop the finding* — and this section points at it rather than re-deriving it. What it adds is only what Google does differently:

- **Two enforcement speeds, and the fast one has no reverse.** Most violations get a documented warning ≥7 days out and a three-strike ladder (3-day hold → 7-day hold → suspension); the *egregious* class (and misrepresentation at its severe end) is suspended on detection with no warning, reaches related accounts on the same payment method, and is rarely reinstated — so the pre-flight screens that no-reverse class **first**. Google's own two-tier enforcement is the reason for the blast-radius ordering rule the social specialist already applies.
- **Misrepresentation is the policy face of the ad-to-landing-page relevance Quality Score already rewards** — dishonest pricing, unavailable offers, unclear relevance, misleading representation read as the same ad-must-be-true-to-its-page coherence this agent chases for cost reasons; the gated demo / "free"-that-isn't is a Rule 2 problem and a policy exposure at once.
- **A competitor's name in ad text is an account-level bet, not a campaign test** — the B2B-search-critical asymmetry: Google does not restrict a rival's trademark as a *keyword* (conquesting bids allowed) but restricts it in *ad text* absent the reseller/informational exception, and the remedy is domain-scoped ("*restrictions will generally be applied on an ongoing basis in any ads that use the same second-level domain in their final URL*"), so a "vs."/"alternatives" headline wagers the whole domain's paid-search presence and belongs at media-plan stage. The exception turns on what the landing page *is*, not on the wording.
- **Restricted/limited categories** flagged with the *product-market-is-not-your-ad-category* trap (embedded fintech, security/background-check tooling), per campaign and per country.

**What is ours:** the two-enforcement-speeds ordering read, the misrepresentation-as-landing-page-relevance reframe, the keyword-vs-ad-text reconciliation with the domain-scoped remedy, and the exception-turns-on-the-destination seam.

**Sourcing & honesty.** Ideas from the same MIT sources the shipped section credits (`gooseworks-ai/goose-skills`, `AgriciDaniel/claude-ads`, `nowork-studio/notfair-plugin`); written from scratch. Every Google position cited to primary policy docs read 2026-08-16 — [Trademarks](https://support.google.com/adspolicy/answer/6118), [Misrepresentation](https://support.google.com/adspolicy/answer/6020955), [What happens if you violate our policies](https://support.google.com/adspolicy/answer/7187501), [account suspensions overview](https://support.google.com/adspolicy/answer/9841640), [Google Ads policies](https://support.google.com/adspolicy/answer/6008942). Only the domain-scope sentence is quoted verbatim; every other Google position is paraphrased-and-cited because it was read through a page summariser (stated in-file). No approval, rejection, appeal-success, or suspension-frequency figure asserted — Google publishes none.

**Verified:** lint 2/2; the two copies are diff-identical; broken-link recheck 0 repo-wide; all six cross-referenced agents (`paid-media-social-ads-specialist`, `paid-media-creative-strategist`, `design-ad-creative-producer`, `pmm-messaging-architect`, `ops-legal-compliance`, `ops-quality-assurance`) exist in both locations; all five new external URLs HTTP 200.

**Deferred:** backlog line 92 (whether the enforcement register is a paid-media or client-ops artifact, and whether `ops-quality-assurance`'s pre-launch checklist needs a platform-policy row) — a maintainer decision, explicitly untouched by this ship. Line 91 marked done.

**The gap, verified in-file:** four decisions read timing/tiers off opens — Rule 6 "send day (Tuesday-Thursday typically highest open rate), send time (8-9am and 12-1pm typically optimal)"; the engagement-tier deliverable "high-engagement subscribers (openers/clickers) receive premium content"; the "optimal send time" deliverable; and the Month-2 A/B "test send time … and send day." Plus Rule 8's inactivity definition "no opens in 60 days," and two Success Metrics — the flagged "Send time optimization impact … 5-15% higher open rate vs. standard 9am send" and the bare "Open rate 25-35%." **Shipped (both dual-located copies, diff-identical):** a new **Critical Rule 9** ("The Send-Time and Engagement-Tier Loop Reads the Reader Off a Proxy, Not the Reader"), a themed section **"Reading the Reader Off a Proxy: Send Time and Engagement Tiers,"** five re-based decision points (Rule 3 subject-line pointer to `email-copywriter`; Rule 6 send day/time → CTA-click-to-session + conversion; Rule 8 inactivity → silence across all human signals; the engagement-tier and send-time deliverables; the Month-2 A/B line), and two corrected metrics (the "5-15%" send-time figure re-based off opens entirely; the Open-rate metric given the name-the-instrument / directional-deliverability-read-only caveat). **The distinct framing** vs. the five prior consumers: this agent is the *literal instance the anchor warns about* — `email-deliverability-specialist` Rule 9 names *send-time optimization* as the canonical proxy-derived decision (MPP prefetches the pixel on its own schedule, so an open timestamp is a relay's clock, not a reader's), and the newsletter's whole optimization loop was built on it. The constructive move is that the newsletter owns confirmed/probable-human signal downstream — the CTA click resolving to a real on-site session, and the MQL conversion, both already tracked — so send-time and tiering move there, with the Rule 9 latency read run first. The tier/sunset error is the damaging one: a proxy opens a dead address forever, so an opens-based sunset never fires for exactly the subscribers it must catch.

**Verified:** `scripts/lint-agents.sh` on both copies — **2/2 pass**; `cp` then `diff` — copies identical; link check re-run — **0 broken** (323); all four referenced agents (`email-deliverability-specialist`, `email-copywriter`, `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`, `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`) exist in-tree; grep confirmed the edit added **no new external URLs** and **no new asserted figures** (one contaminated benchmark figure was removed, not added). **Deferred:** nothing on line 86 — the item is now **fully complete, all six consumers re-based**; checkbox flipped to `[x]`.

The **distinct framing** vs. the three email consumers: this is the only surface with a native *confirmed-human* signal opens lack — the reply — so the reframe is constructive rather than merely cautionary; the send-time read is uniquely broken because scanners prefetch within seconds of delivery, making a "best open time" mostly the SDR's own send clock echoed back; and the Response Rate fix also repairs a pre-existing internal inconsistency. Consuming agent references `email-deliverability-specialist` Rule 9 for the mechanism/tiers and keeps lead-scoring math with `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`; no new external claims or figures.

So it was the correct next single item, and it stays inside the same email thread. One file, its two dual-located copies.

**The gap, verified in-file:** `email-copywriter` decides subject-line A/B tests on **open rate** and twice declares a winner on a **fixed percentage gap** — "requires ≥10% difference to be significant" (A/B protocol) and "requiring 10%+ performance difference for significance" (copy-testing protocol). Two independent errors stack: (1) the subject line's whole job is to move opens, which is the *most* machine-contaminated signal (privacy proxies + security scanners fetch the pixel with no human), so of every consumer this agent optimizes the number machines generate most; (2) a percentage gap is not a significance criterion at all — significance is a function of sample size and variance, so a fixed-gap rule crowns noise on small sends and misses real effects on large ones. A test sized against a contaminated open rate is additionally under-powered, so "no winner" is the *expected* outcome even when a real one exists. Grep confirmed the file had **zero** prior mention of proxy/MPP/bot/contamination.

**Shipped (both dual-located copies, diff-identical):** a new **Critical Rule 9** ("A Subject-Line Winner Is Not a Percent Gap in Opens"), a themed section **"Testing Subject Lines on a Signal Machines Fake,"** three re-based decision points (the A/B protocol → decide on the least-fakeable signal the send can power and route the verdict through the trust discipline; the copy-testing protocol → drop the fixed-gap rule; the sample-size calculator → size against the minimum detectable effect and the metric actually decided on), and two metric caveats (Subject Line Performance → name the instrument, a winner clears the trust gate; Copy Testing Wins → a logged no-difference is learning, most B2B subject-line tests won't resolve a winner).

**What is ours vs. borrowed.** A *consuming* agent again: the contamination mechanism and the four evidence tiers live in `email-deliverability-specialist` Rule 9, and the general experiment-trust discipline (peeking/optional-stopping, sample-ratio mismatch, the ship/no-difference/extend verdict) lives in `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`'s *Trust the Split Before the Winner* — both **referenced, not re-derived**, so this run asserts **no new external claims and no new figures**. The distinct contribution is this agent's and specific to it: it is the only consumer running a *formal significance test*, so its failure is a **test-validity** one (wrong metric × invalid significance rule), where the two prior consumers' were an *action* (automation routing) and a *billing* (newsletter sponsor) failure. Correcting the fixed-percent-gap statistics is the piece owned by no other agent.

**Verified before claiming done:** `scripts/lint-agents.sh` on both copies → **2/2 pass**; the two copies are **diff-identical** (`cp` then `diff`); repo-wide broken-link check re-run → **0 broken**; both referenced agents (`email-deliverability-specialist`, `analytics-conversion-rate-optimizer`) confirmed to exist; grep confirmed the edit added **no new external URLs** and **no new asserted figures**.

**Deferred (one change per run):** the last two consumers on this item — the send-time reads in `content/content-newsletter-curator` ("5-15% higher open rate") and `sales/sales-outbound-strategist` ("Tuesday-Thursday 9am-11am for best open rates"), both keying timing off proxy-fetch timestamps. Item updated to **PARTLY WORKED 2026-08-13** with those two as the remaining head.

**What is ours vs. borrowed.** This is a *consuming* agent: the evidence tiers (confirmed-human / probable-human / unconfirmed / silent), the contamination mechanics (MPP, Safe Links), and every primary-source citation live in `email-deliverability-specialist`'s Rule 9 and are **referenced, not re-derived** — so this run asserts **no new external claims and no new figures**. The distinct framing is automation-specific and ours: deliverability's worst case is a lingering dead address, this agent's is a *poisoned forecast and a wasted sales call* (an action on a machine event spends real resources and corrupts the conversion data the model is tuned on); the false-negative re-engagement direction; and the MQL threshold as the one irreversible, human-reaching action that only Probable-human-or-above may cross. Seams drawn: the scoring-model math stays with `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`, and the single-provider open-collapse anomaly routes to `email-deliverability-specialist`.

The section names **review gating** (screening the public-review ask behind an NPS/CSAT score) as the market default and takes the honest posture: the rule does not ban it, the FTC says it "could violate the FTC Act," so it is a *named exposure routed to counsel, not a settled ban*. Three disciplines — **timing vs. screening** (timing to a lifecycle high point is Rule 4, fine; screening *who* by predicted rating is the exposure), the **incentive bright line** (§465.4, a sentiment-conditioned incentive is a plain violation by the rule's text), and **no rating-based suppression** (§465.7(b) display rule + its equally-applied carve-out; §465.7(a) threats bar; contacting an unhappy reviewer is permitted, paying to delete is not).

**Primary-source verification (the backlog demanded it):** eCFR and both Federal Register / FTC HTML+PDF routes were bot-blocked (302→unblock host, or 403), so verified against the **official CFR PDF** on govinfo ([16 CFR §§465.4-465.7, 1-1-25 ed.](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2025-title16-vol1/pdf/CFR-2025-title16-vol1-sec465-6.pdf), read via saved PDF) for the regulatory text, and the **FTC business-guidance Q&A** (fetched with a browser UA; verbatim: *"The rule does not contain a specific prohibition against such conduct. But this practice could violate the FTC Act"*; effective date *"October 21, 2024"*; the contact-to-resolve and pay-to-take-down statements) for the solicitation nuance. The gating-is-unlawful reading is **flagged unsettled, not asserted**; §465.4 and §465.7 are quoted as the bright lines they are. Ideas-only; source `the-nam-shub/e5-real-skills` is **unlicensed — no content reused**; the timing-vs-screening framing, bright-line-vs-gray-area split, and the seam are ours. No enforcement/penalty figure asserted.

### 2026-08-12 — the Customer Advocacy Manager learns that its roster is not its capacity (automated skill-scout run)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration — scout the market, compare, act on the single highest-value opportunity.

**Discipline chosen by rotation: PMM / sales / GTM.** Last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-08 (the 08-09 recovery run closed its stakeholder-map item), the oldest of the four — email/analytics/ops and content/SEO-AEO-GEO both ran 08-10, paid/social 08-11. Its queue entered this run holding only two open **SCOPE QUESTIONs** awaiting a maintainer decision (pricing/packaging ownership, now five sightings; the grade-the-function persona) plus the claim-provenance item, which carries the advisory-vs-executable architecture caveat this repo has declined a dozen times — i.e. no actionable evidence-blocked item.

**Scouted:** 12 sources across repository search, path search on advocacy vocabulary, and a by-file-tree re-check of the tracked PMM collections. Full verdicts in [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**The thesis, sighted three times independently in one run.** [lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth](https://github.com/lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth) (MIT, B2B-SaaS-shaped, pushed 08-10) states it most directly — *advocates are not a renewable resource* — routing every request AE → customer marketing → CSM → advocate, capping requests per advocate, and treating burnout as a **revoked status**. [LeadMagic/gtm-skills](https://github.com/LeadMagic/gtm-skills) (MIT) names over-using the same three references as a core pitfall and carries `last_advocacy_ask` and `reference_calls_ytd` as **CRM fields** — the ask log as tracked state, not advice. [the-nam-shub/e5-real-skills](https://github.com/the-nam-shub/e5-real-skills) (**unlicensed — ideas only**) reaches the same constraint from the review corner, staggering platform asks across months so no individual is overwhelmed. **Correction worth recording:** LeadMagic was declared "fully worked" on 2026-07-28 — that mine was of its *sales* corner; its `growth/` tree had never been assessed.

**Gap grep-verified before writing.** `advocate fatigue`, `over-request`, `request cap`, `cap requests`, `depletable`, `reference fatigue`, `shared resource` = **zero** repo-wide; `burnout` resolved only to *internal team capacity* in `pm-resource-allocator` / `pm-client-success-manager`; `CSM` appeared repo-wide only in `pmm-launch-manager`, `customer success manager` nowhere; `logo rights` / `quote approval` / `approval to publish` = zero, the single adjacent line being `content-case-study-producer`'s per-asset "permission to publish." The two most-mined PMM sources (`stefanoskarakasis`, `pmalliance`) were re-checked **by file tree** and carry no advocacy path at all — the discipline lives in *growth* and *customer-marketing* collections, not PMM ones.

**Target: `pmm-customer-advocacy` — 71 lines, never enhanced since the first pass, the smallest PMM agent.** It modeled advocacy as recruitment and inventory end to end: Rule 5 sets a roster target, Rule 6 grades account health by churn risk, Rule 2's friction discipline governs the size of *one* ask and never the sum, Rule 4's lifecycle timing is a program-side calendar with no relationship-side veto. **The finding that made it worth the run is in our own metrics:** "40-60 reference calls per quarter" against "5-10 actively available" advocates is **4-12 asks per person per quarter** — several times any published practitioner cap. The agent stated the roster-consuming quota as a goal.

**Shipped** (both dual-located copies): **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Budgeting the Ask: Advocate Capacity and Depletion"** section, an **Advocate Capacity Plan & Ask Ledger** deliverable, four rewritten/added success metrics, and a corrected **Rule 3**. Eight layers are ours: the capacity arithmetic (with the old metric named in-file as the worked example of the failure); the per-person rather than per-account ledger; ask weighting, since a cap counted in *asks* invites substituting heavy ones for light; the champion-job-change over-count, with the seam to `sales-outbound-strategist`'s job-change triggers drawn so the same event is not double-used; the relationship-owner veto recorded with a reason and a re-ask date; recorded states rather than silences, the repo's standing *unknown never rounds to available* applied to a person; the latency tell; the recipient's-policy framing of compensation; and the concentration / never-asked pair. **Rule 3 corrected in passing** — it prescribed a flat cash schedule one rule away from Rule 7's "never incentivize reviews," with no line drawn between compensating a *reference call* and compensating a *public review*.

**Sources & honesty.** Ideas-only, written from scratch; no prose adapted from any source, and the unlicensed source contributed corroboration only. **The sources' per-quarter cap numbers (1, or 2-3) were deliberately NOT adopted as a rule** — practitioner defaults with no measurement behind them; the file instructs setting and recording the cap. G2's incentive policy quoted and cited to [G2's Community Guidelines](https://legal.g2.com/community-guidelines) (read 2026-08-12): the $100 USD cap, the incentivized label, that eligibility is "never based on the opinions, positive or negative, expressed in the review," and the disclose-to-G2 requirement. The gift-policy reasoning is labeled in-file as our judgment, not a measured finding. **No burnout rate, decline rate, or advocacy-attributed revenue figure asserted.**

**Verified.** Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical (71 → 102 lines); all four new external URLs HTTP 200; both internal agent references (`sales-outbound-strategist`, `ops-legal-compliance`) resolve to real files.

**Deferred to backlog** (one change per run): **review-solicitation integrity** — Rule 7 says "comply with FTC review guidelines" and stops, and `16 CFR` / `Part 465` / `review gating` / `review suppression` / `Endorsement Guide` are zero repo-wide, with the e5 source teaching NPS-gated review solicitation that our agent neither endorses nor warns against; and a **scoped, expiring consent record** for advocacy assets (what they approved, on which surfaces, for how long — distinct from *how often* you ask). Both filed with their seams to decide first. One change shipped; stopped.

The freshest teed-up P2 curation note was [backlog.md](backlog.md) line 120 part **(b)** — aaron-he-zhu's `bid-strategy-planner` against the PPC Strategist's bidding ground — explicitly deferred to a future paid run by today's fourth run, which shipped part (a). Part (b) required a gap grep-verify first.

**The source, read in full.** Fetched `ad/orchestrate/bid-strategy-planner/SKILL.md` and its `references/bid-strategy-matrix.md` (Apache-2.0). Its method: conversion **volume decides which strategy is viable** (~30/mo → tCPA, ~15/mo with value → tROAS, below → max-conversions/manual — all labeled *its own* rules of thumb); the **starting target is anchored to achievable, not aspirational** (a target the account never hit throttles delivery); and **thin campaigns pool into a portfolio bid strategy** to learn on combined signal. Its learning-phase-entry half was already shipped as part (a) hours earlier, so only the *strategy-selection + starting-target* half was live.

**Three gaps, grep-verified distinct from part (a).** (1) The agent mentioned conversion volume **only for learning-phase *exit*** (line 81), never as the pre-launch floor that decides which strategy you can pick. (2) It derived tCPA from **LTV — the affordable ceiling — and never anchored the *starting* target to trailing-achieved performance**; the whole "too-low target throttles delivery" discipline was absent. (3) `portfolio bid` / `bid portfolio` / pooled-signal existed in the repo **only as the budget optimizer's budget-pooling *hazard*** (line 100), never as the *bidding* mechanic of pooling a thin campaign's signal. All three cohere into one upstream idea the learning-phase section does not touch: *an automated bid strategy is only as good as the conversion signal feeding it.*

**Owner: `paid-media-ppc-strategist`** — the bidding owner; the new section sits directly under *Operating a Live Account*, after the learning-phase subsection it builds on (that one reads a live automated strategy; this one chooses it and sets its target before launch).

**Shipped** (both dual-located copies): **Critical Rule 10**, a **"Signal before strategy: what your conversion volume lets you bid"** subsection, an extended **Bidding Strategy Framework** deliverable, and a signal-fit-bidding success metric. **The reconciliation is the load-bearing move and it is ours:** the agent's identity says "bid strategy should reflect customer LTV" — kept intact by making **LTV the *ceiling* and trailing-achieved performance the *starting point*** (start at achievable, step toward the LTV ceiling after each learning exit), so the new discipline extends the identity rather than contradicting it. Also ours: the **B2B inversion** (a demo-request campaign can take a quarter to clear a floor a consumer campaign clears in a day → the floor is the binding constraint, not a checkbox), and the **pool-the-signal-not-the-budget** seam (a *portfolio bid strategy* pools conversion signal and helps a thin campaign; a *shared budget* pools money and — per the budget optimizer — leaks it to the broadest campaign; different objects, so pool the signal without the budget). The too-thin-even-to-pool case hands off to the higher-funnel-event move the learning-phase section already owns, not restated.

**Sources & honesty.** Google figures quoted from primary docs read 2026-08-11: [About Target CPA bidding](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/6268632) ("we recommend you measure performance for the last 30 days, including at least 30 conversions"; "setting a target that's too low ... may cause you to forgo clicks that could result in conversions, resulting in fewer total conversions") and Google's [Target ROAS requirements](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/6268637) ("at least 15 conversions in the past 30 days" on Search and Shopping). The 30-conversion tCPA figure is flagged in-file as Google's *evaluation recommendation*, not a hard activation gate, and every volume threshold is marked directional. Discipline surfaced by [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (Apache-2.0), which labels its own thresholds and its ≤15%-per-step rule as rules of thumb — ideas only, written from scratch, no text adapted; Apache-2.0 notice requirements would apply to any direct adaptation and none was made. **No CPA, ROAS, or conversion-volume figure of our own asserted.**

**Verified.** Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; broken-link scan re-run post-edit, 0 broken; the two new Google URLs fetched live this run, the aaron GitHub URL already in-repo.

**Deferred.** Part **(b)**'s remaining sibling — aaron's `bid-strategy-planner` *portfolio-grouping* and switch-timing tables beyond what shipped — is fully covered by this section; the aaron `ad/` mine's **last open thread is now the `bid-strategy-planner` item itself, closed here.** Backlog 120 retains one genuinely open sub-item only if a future run finds new ground; marked done for (a)+(b). One item shipped; stopped.

**The gap, grep-verified.** Today's third run (the budget-optimizer pacing ship) left a **"Learning reset"** row that reads a pacing signal as "observational only" inside a reset, and `paid-media-creative-strategist` has long carried a **"Respect the platform's learning period"** test caveat — but each is a *local caveat*, and **no agent owned the state itself**. Confirmed: `learning limited`, `learning status`, `conversion cycle`, and `re-enters learning` all returned **zero** hits repo-wide; the only `learning`-phase mentions were the two caveats above plus the PPC spend-change gate's *one variable, one verification window* rule, which needs — but never states — a reason its clock cannot start at the moment of the change.

**Owner: `paid-media-ppc-strategist`.** It holds the spend-change gate, the authorization mechanism for the exact edits (bid-strategy switches, target/budget changes) that re-enter learning, and it is the account-operations agent. Making it the owner gives the two existing caveats a home to defer to — the repo's established owner-plus-cross-reference seam.

**Shipped** (both dual-located copies — `paid-media/` and `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/paid-media-ops/agents/`): **Critical Rule 9**, a **"The learning phase: when the number isn't real yet"** subsection placed right after the spend-change gate, and a learning-state success metric. Platform mechanics cited to primary docs; **three disciplines are ours**: (1) **the recursion** — the fix for a pacing/CAC gap is itself a significant edit, so you cannot repair the number and read the repair in the same window, and that measurement blackout is part of the cost of making the change; (2) **the doom loop** — reacting to a bad in-learning read with another edit resets learning again, so no material edit and no verdict until the window closes; (3) the **B2B conversion-volume inversion** — Meta's ~50-events-in-a-week exit is a consumer-volume criterion a B2B ad set optimized on demo requests may never reach ("Learning limited" indefinitely), so set the verification window to the real conversion cycle and, where the primary event is too rare, optimize on a higher-funnel event and validate down-funnel value separately (the same move `paid-media-attribution-analyst` makes on a sparse terminal metric). Authorization stays with the gate; this section governs only when the number becomes real.

**Sources & honesty.** Discipline (learning phase as a first-class *state* that gates action) surfaced by aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills (Apache-2.0) — ideas only, written from scratch, no text adapted; Apache-2.0 notice requirements would apply to any direct adaptation and none was made. Google facts quoted from two primary docs read 2026-08-11: [Duration of the learning period](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/13020501) ("up to 3 weeks or 1-2 conversion cycles") and [About bid strategy statuses](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/6263057) (the three "Learning" reasons; "you may not want to measure performance until the learning period is over"). Meta's [About the learning phase](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/112167992830700) / [About learning limited](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/269269737396981) / [Significant edits and learning phase](https://www.facebook.com/business/help/316478108955072) pages **render as JavaScript to an automated fetch** (a limitation this repo logged on 2026-07-30), so the ~50-events/week exit and the significant-edit list are taken from Meta's Help Center text as surfaced in search 2026-08-11, flagged as such in-file. **No spend, CAC, or learning-duration figure of our own asserted.**

**Verified.** Lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; broken-link scan re-run post-edit, 0 broken; all new external URLs (2 Google fetched live, 3 Meta confirmed as live Help Center URLs, 1 GitHub already in-repo) resolve.
