### 2026-08-11 (third run dated 2026-08-11) — the Budget Optimizer learns that a plan is not a spend (automated skill-scout run)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration — market intelligence and adoption, distinct from the general maintenance routine. Do one high-value thing, or nothing.

**Discipline chosen by rotation: paid media / social.** Last the *focus* discipline on 2026-08-07, the oldest of the four (PMM/sales/GTM 08-08, email/analytics/ops 08-10, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-10 second run). Its queue entered the run effectively empty — the `olson-adam/ad-mastermind` mine closed 08-07, the CPL-vs-CRM item 08-03, and three of the four `social-media-skills/skills` slices are worked (08-07, 08-08, 08-09), leaving only that repo's freshness-convention *architecture* question, which is a maintainer call.

**Scouted:** 14 sources (see [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md) for every one with its verdict). The run's thesis came from a GitHub *code* search rather than a repo search, and the first four hits for pacing vocabulary were mirrors and registries re-publishing one primary source — the same lesson as 08-10, that hit counts in this market measure re-publication, not coverage.

**The find, and the gap.** Three independent sources converge on the same claim: **pacing is a discipline distinct from allocation**, each drawing an explicit seam back to its own budget optimizer — [aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills](https://github.com/aaron-he-zhu/aaron-marketing-skills) (Apache-2.0, 2.5k★, `ad/scale/budget-pacing-monitor`, a source on `watch` since 07-30 for its *email* corpus whose **ad** corner had never been mined), [logly/mureo](https://github.com/logly/mureo) (Apache-2.0, "trajectory management, not allocation"), and [scumunna/programmatic-skills](https://github.com/scumunna/programmatic-skills) (MIT, the Google-Ads-vs-DV360 platform mechanics). Gap grep-verified before writing: **`underdeliver`, `under-deliver`, `overdeliver`, `over-deliver`, `budget cap`, `shared budget`, `burn rate`, `flighting`, `learning phase`, `budget constrained`, and `spend delivery` all returned zero hits repo-wide**, and `pacing` in the paid sense resolved to three real hits — a *frequency*-pacing line in `paid-media-programmatic-buyer` (a different discipline) and two blurb bullets in `paid-media-ops/SKILL.md`. The pattern the ledger keeps recording: **the control named in a metrics list, with no instrument and no owner** — and the market's own best-resourced collection does the same thing (`AgriciDaniel/claude-ads` advertises pacing three times in its budget skill's routing description and its skill body carries no pacing method at all).

**Owner: `paid-media-budget-optimizer`** — 61 lines, the thinnest paid agent, **never enhanced since the first pass**, and the last of the two flagged as such on 08-07. It mandated empirical diminishing-returns curves (Rule 2), quarterly reoptimization (Rule 5), and scenario forecasts (Rule 3) — every one fit over *delivered* spend — with no way to ask whether the plan it modeled was ever executed.

**Shipped** (both dual-located copies — `paid-media/` and `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/paid-media-ops/agents/`): **Critical Rule 9**, a **"Planned Is Not Delivered: Reading Pacing Before the Curve"** section, a **Budget Delivery & Pacing Report** deliverable, and three rewritten success metrics. **The organizing idea is ours and it is a validity precondition, not ops hygiene:** raise a budget and watch returns flatten — that is what saturation looks like, and it is exactly what a *delivery constraint* looks like, because money a channel cannot absorb returns nothing either. The chart cannot separate them; only the delivery record can. So **a saturation threshold observed at a spend level the channel never exceeded is the top of the observed range**, and under-delivery rounds to *unknown above here*, never to saturated — the repo's standing "unknown never rounds to pass," applied to a curve. Five further layers are ours: the **B2B inversion** (the market's pacing writing is consumer-shaped and fears burning the day's budget by noon; in B2B the binding constraint is the *audience*, so a channel at 70% of plan has a reach ceiling and needs reallocation, not a bid increase); **a ceiling is not a plan** and **the daily number is an average, not a cap**, so a single heavy day is not an incident; **underspend as an unplanned reallocation** with three destinations that all rewrite the designed allocation (expired at close / pooled by a shared budget to the broadest campaign that *can* absorb it / a period-end catch-up buying the worst inventory at its worst price); **"Limited by budget" is a recommendation with an interest** (Google fires it at "5% or more of your potential traffic" and documents raising the budget as the remedy — and potential traffic is not qualified traffic); and the **six-driver table** where the remedies do not overlap and *unknown never rounds to saturated*.

**Seams drawn in-file, three of them:** *authorization* for any budget move already belongs to `paid-media-ppc-strategist`'s spend-change gate (07-26/08-07) — this section supplies only the *reason*; flight-level DSP pacing (insertion-order totals, even/ahead modes) stays with `paid-media-programmatic-buyer`; and a perfectly-paced channel with no incrementality evidence is a well-delivered buy of *unknown value*, handed back to Rule 4 and `paid-media-attribution-analyst` (the same precondition framing as 08-07).

**Two fabricated-precision metrics fixed in passing** (same call as 07-30, 08-05, 08-06, 08-07, 08-08, 08-10): ">85% accuracy in scenario revenue forecasts vs. actual results" and "diminishing returns thresholds for 80%+ of channels with <10% variance vs. modeled results" both grade the model's confidence in numbers the new section says it has no right to. Replaced by a curve-validity metric (zero saturation thresholds asserted above a channel's delivered range), a plan-vs-delivered reconciliation metric, and an in-period pacing cadence (a gap first found at period close is a gap that could no longer be fixed).

**Licensing.** Ideas-only, written from scratch; two of the three sources are Apache-2.0, whose notice requirements would apply to any direct adaptation — none was made. Every platform figure quoted from and cited to a primary source read 2026-08-11: Google Ads (2× daily / 30.4× monthly; budget pacing insights; shared budgets), LinkedIn (+50% on a given day; lifetime budget never exceeded), Meta Marketing API (+25% on a day). **No under-delivery rate, waste percentage, or reallocation-uplift figure asserted.**

**Verified:** lint 2/2; copies diff-identical; 296 internal `.md` links checked repo-wide, 0 broken; all new external URLs live.

**Deferred:** the rest of aaron-he-zhu's `ad/` corpus is on `watch` — its **learning-phase-as-a-gated-state** idea and its bid-strategy ground, both to be grep-verified before any future action. `portermetricsample/marketing-skills` ships an on-thesis Google Ads pacing skill but asserts no licence; flagged for a licence re-check on a future paid run.

The top *unblocked* item is the **(b)** slice of the [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) (MIT) watch item — the `seo-traffic-diagnosis` hypothesis-before-fix discipline — whose own note demanded an **overlap analysis first** because "it may itself resolve as a non-gap."

**Overlap analysis — the note's own instruction, run first, split the item.** Read the source skill in full (`skills/seo-traffic-diagnosis/SKILL.md` + `references/diagnosis-checklist.md`). Its five-layer **method** (confirm-real → localize → page → technical → external; single-sentence hypothesis; contradicting-evidence discipline) **resolved as a non-gap**: `analytics-performance-analyst`'s "Reading a Change Before Explaining It" already owns the site-level method (candidate mechanisms, evidence *against* each, the smallest read-only distinguishing check, `unresolved` as a terminal verdict), and `seo-content-optimizer`'s decay triage owns the page-level version (the four-signature grid, the impostor rule-out, the site-wide-cause check that routes away). Rebuilding either would duplicate an existing owner — forbidden. **But the flagged area held a real adjacent gap** (same shape as the 08-10 and 08-11 runs): the SEO-native *sitewide cause taxonomy* the method runs over. Grep-verified — `manual action`, `core update`, and `algorithm update` returned **zero** hits repo-wide; the one `security issue` hit is an SSL/mixed-content audit line, not a diagnostic — so the two most SEO-native sitewide causes (a Google algorithm/core update and a manual action or security issue) were owned by nobody.

**Owner decided: `seo-technical-auditor`.** It is the site-level SEO forensic specialist, is already the destination `analytics-performance-analyst` routes migrations to, and already owns two of the branches a sitewide-drop differential routes into (the indexing-disposition section, the migration section). The differential is the natural *router above them* — the triage that decides *which* branch (or an algo update, a manual action, a deploy regression, a SERP-layout shift, or falling demand) you are in — so it ties the agent's existing sections together and fills the two holes. Distinct capability, not a third pass on the same ground: migration is one branch, indexing is another, this is the front door that sorts a not-yet-diagnosed sitewide drop into them. `analytics-performance-analyst` (marketing generalist, routes SEO out) and `seo-content-optimizer` (page-level, routes site-level away) were both wrong owners; a new persona is a proposal, not an autonomous ship.

**Shipped** (both dual-located copies — `seo/` and `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/seo-growth/agents/`): **Critical Rule 11**, a **"The Whole Site Dropped: Name the Class Before You Audit"** section, a **Sitewide Traffic-Drop Diagnosis** deliverable, and a **sitewide-drop class-before-fix** success metric. The section: confirm-real deferred to `analytics-performance-analyst` + `seo-content-optimizer`, not rebuilt; a **localization table** where the segment names the owner, foregrounding the **branded-vs-non-branded cut** (a branded-clicks collapse is a brand/outage/manual-action event, *not* an SEO ranking problem); the **two report-read causes** — Manual Actions + Security Issues checked **first** because they are the one cause with a binary answer, remedy = a reconsideration request (`"select Request Review"`) not a content refresh or a crawl audit; the **algorithm-update** read held honest by *timing-is-a-hypothesis* (rule out a same-window deploy before accepting the update) and *there-is-no-switch-to-flip* (a core update is broad, "don't target specific sites," recovery "waiting until the next core update" — the content owners' slow work); a **deploy-is-the-prime-suspect** read for a sudden cliff (routing / robots / `noindex` / render regression, the §indexing and §migration mechanisms shipped by an ordinary release); a fixed **certainty-and-cost ordering** (measurement → manual action → deploy → migration → algorithm → demand) that puts the over-called external causes last; and a **two-classes-both-fit** guardrail that carries honest uncertainty forward rather than forcing a clean conclusion.

**Seam drawn in-file:** the diagnostic *method* is explicitly `analytics-performance-analyst`'s and is deferred to, not rebuilt — this section supplies only the SEO cause taxonomy that method runs over — so the two agents never contradict on "how to diagnose a move." Page-level decay signatures route to `seo-content-optimizer`; SERP-interception to `seo-ai-search-optimizer` (via its existing ownership); a genuine competitor share-shift to `pmm-competitive-intelligence`.

**Attribution/licensing:** ideas-only, written from scratch; the five-layer frame, the branded-vs-non-branded localizer, and the "don't jump to the algorithm" failure pattern credited in-file to the `seo-traffic-diagnosis` skill in `rampstackco/claude-skills` (MIT, license verified via GitHub API this run — 529★, pushed 2026-08-10). Ours: the class-before-fix framing, the certainty-and-cost ordering, the deploy-is-the-prime-suspect read, the branded-cut-first emphasis, and the two-classes-both-fit guardrail. Manual-action, security-issue, and core-update behavior quoted from and cited to Google primary documentation read 2026-08-11: [Manual Actions report](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/9044175), [Security Issues report](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/9044101), [Google Search's core updates](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/appearance/core-updates), and the dated [Search Status Dashboard](https://status.search.google.com/products/rGHU1u87FJnkP6W2GwMi/history). **No traffic-loss, recovery-time, or ranking figure is asserted anywhere in the section.**

**Verified:** lint **2/2** on both copies; copies **diff-identical** (250 lines each; synced by copy, not hand-duplicated); 0 broken internal `.md` links (294 checked, re-run after edits); the 6 new concept markers present identically in both copies; all four Google source URLs + the rampstackco repo return HTTP 200.

### 2026-08-11 — the Technical SEO Auditor learns to run a site migration, closing a dead-ending inbound handoff (automated maintenance run)

**Job:** [ROUTINE.md](ROUTINE.md), one iteration. Health check first; then the top *unblocked* backlog item.

The `2026-04-03` CATALYST stamps are v1.0 version footers, not freshness commitments. No P0.

**Item pulled.** All P1 items remain blocked (native subagents #1; proposals #2 + `ops-legal-compliance`; distribution behind human-written / in-app-form / ≥40-star gates; the SCOPE-QUESTION items await maintainer decisions, not evidence). The top *unblocked* item is the [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) (MIT) content/SEO watch item, whose first-listed sub-opportunity (a) was a migration capability for `seo-technical-auditor` — "grep-verify first."

**Gap grep-verified — and it was sharper than the note said.** `migration`/`redirect map`/`replatform` appeared only *incidentally* in the auditor (a security-list mention, a single-page 301 row, the index table) — never as a capability. But the grep also surfaced that **`analytics-performance-analyst` line 112 routes "site migration, redirect, canonical, or template change" *to* `seo-technical-auditor`**, and both `seo-content-optimizer` (line 56, "a domain or URL migration" as a decline-impostor) and `seo-ai-search-optimizer` (line 137, re-platform/CDN migration flips crawler access) name migrations as events they route away. So the owner had no content and the inbound handoff **dead-ended** — the "control asserted, instrument absent" pattern, plus a broken seam. Note: `seo-technical-auditor` was enhanced hours earlier (the indexing-disposition section, run directly below) — this is a *distinct* capability (whole-site move vs. per-page index decision), not a second pass on the same ground.

**Shipped** (both dual-located copies — `seo/` and `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/seo-growth/agents/`): **Critical Rule 10**, an **"A Migration Moves Every Signal at Once"** section, a **Site Migration Runbook** deliverable, and a **migration-signal-retention** success metric. The section: the single-page 301 rule from §5 scaled to every URL at once; **baseline-before-cutover** as the only line between an expected reprocessing dip and a real regression ("you cannot fix, or even detect, what you did not record"); the **one-to-one 301 map** measured by its orphans; the **one-hop rule** ("Google follows up to 10 redirect hops" reframed as a survival limit, not a design budget); **move-all-at-once + release the staging `noindex`/robots block** (the §3 belt-and-braces trap at its most expensive, since a `Disallow` left on the old host stops Google seeing the redirects); a **don't-fold-a-redesign-or-rewrite-into-the-move** rule so a traffic move stays attributable (ours, the isolate-the-variable discipline `analytics-performance-analyst` needs on the receiving end); the **weeks-to-reprocess / redirects-stay-≥1-year** windows from Google's own numbers; and a **move-type blast-radius table** (HTTPS / URL-structure / domain / replatform / consolidation).

**Seam closed in-file:** the section explicitly names itself the landing point for `analytics-performance-analyst`'s routing, and routes any post-move ranking loss on a clean-`301` URL onward to `seo-content-optimizer` with the technical field cleared — so the handoff that previously dead-ended now runs to a destination and back out.

**Attribution/licensing:** ideas-only, written from scratch; the checklist scaffold and the migration-as-highest-risk framing credited in-file to `rampstackco/claude-skills` (MIT, license verified via GitHub API this run). **Its uncited "30–70% traffic loss" figure was deliberately NOT adopted** — no migration loss or recovery percentage is asserted anywhere in the section. Ours: the baseline-is-the-only-line read, the ten-hop-is-a-survival-limit framing, the don't-fold-a-rewrite-into-the-move rule, and the blast-radius table. Every redirect behavior, timeframe, and procedure quoted from and cited to Google primary docs read 2026-08-10: [Redirects and Google Search](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/301-redirects), [Site moves with URL changes](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/site-move-with-url-changes), [HTTP status codes and network errors](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/http-network-errors).

**Verified:** lint **2/2** on both copies; copies **diff-identical** (186 lines each; synced by copy, not hand-duplicated); 0 broken internal `.md` links (289 checked, re-run after edits); the 4 new concept markers present 4/4 in both copies; all three Google source URLs + the source repo return HTTP 200.

### 2026-08-10 (second run) — Skill Scout (content/SEO-AEO-GEO rotation): the Technical SEO Auditor learns that indexing is a decision, not a delivery (automated maintenance run)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration on today's rotation discipline. Second scout run today; the first (email/analytics/ops) is logged directly below.

**Rotation:** content/SEO-AEO-GEO was last the *focus* discipline on **2026-08-06** — the oldest of the four (paid/social 08-07, PMM/sales/GTM 08-08, email/analytics/ops earlier today; 08-09 was a recovery + AEO/GEO freshness run, not a survey). Its queue entered this run holding only the `seo-programmatic-strategist` PROPOSAL ([#2](https://github.com/shalintripathi/saas-marketing-agents/issues/2)), which is blocked on a maintainer scope decision rather than more evidence. So: a fresh survey.

**Scouted** (content/SEO-AEO-GEO focus): GitHub search across SEO / content-marketing / AEO-GEO skill repos pushed since mid-2026, plus a GitHub *code* search for who, if anyone, owns the indexation-decision vocabulary. Notable first sightings: [rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills) (524★, MIT, pushed 2026-08-10) — the best-organized SEO collection surveyed to date, 16 SEO skills each with a `references/` set; [oegeyilmaz9/seo-aeo-geo-ultimate](https://github.com/oegeyilmaz9/seo-aeo-geo-ultimate) (Apache-2.0, evidence-contract architecture); plus re-checks of the tracked AEO/GEO sources. **The find is [kostja94/marketing-skills](https://github.com/kostja94/marketing-skills)** (875★, **MIT**, pushed 2026-06-09) — dismissed on 2026-07-21 for its *social* skills, but its `skills/seo/technical/indexing` skill sits on ground we had **nothing** on.

**Gap grep-verified before writing:** `not indexed`, `Page indexing`, `URL Inspection`, `X-Robots-Tag`, `nofollow`, `noindex,follow`, `redirect chain`, `410`, and `Removals` returned **zero** hits repo-wide; `soft 404` appeared exactly twice — both of them the *same* promise, in a `seo-technical-auditor` deliverable that names "soft 404 diagnosis" and supplies no method. So the agent (**52 lines, the smallest in the repo, never enhanced since the first pass**) modeled indexing as a plumbing outcome: every remediation lever it holds (robots, canonicals, sitemaps, crawl budget, rendering) governs *eligibility*, and it had no branch at all for the state Google documents as `Crawled - currently not indexed` — fetched cleanly and declined — against which all of them are no-ops. Its own Success Metric nonetheless promised "95%+ of target pages indexed within 30 days." Same pattern the ledger keeps recording (LinkedIn delivery 08-03, content decay 08-02, entity recognition 08-06, supply quality 08-07, launch risk 08-08, experiment trust earlier today): **the control asserted, the instrument absent.** Confirmed the market does not own it either — `rampstackco`'s `seo-technical` Indexability layer covers *signals you send* (canonical/noindex hygiene) and never the engine's own refusal, and its `seo-traffic-diagnosis` and `seo-site-health-audit` have no not-indexed content.

**Shipped** (both dual-located copies — `seo/` and `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/seo-growth/agents/`): **Critical Rule 9**, an **"Indexed Is a Decision, Not a Delivery"** section, an **Index Disposition Audit** deliverable, and a rewritten indexation success metric. The section: a **four-disposition model** (intended exclusion / broken signal / deferred crawl / declined) mapping each Search Console state to an owner instead of one "pages not indexed" defect count; the **declined bucket has no technical lever, so route it** to `seo-content-optimizer` and `content-blog-strategist` with the technical field explicitly cleared first; the **robots.txt-cancels-noindex trap** (stacking a `Disallow` on a `noindex` removes the only directive that was working) with `X-Robots-Tag` for non-HTML; the **intended-exclusion map** declared before the audit so intentional exclusions stop resurfacing as findings each cycle; a removal-mechanism table; and **unknown never rounds to indexed**.

**Seam drawn in-file:** `seo-content-optimizer`'s impostor check already sends indexation questions *here* before doing content work — this section is the return leg, so the handoff runs in both directions instead of dead-ending.

**Two staleness bugs fixed in passing** (a scout run is allowed to correct what it trips over): **(a) FID.** `INP` returned **zero** hits repo-wide while `FID`/`First Input Delay` appeared in five places, i.e. the repo taught a Core Web Vital Google **retired on 2024-09-09** (INP replaced it 2024-03-12) and prescribed a threshold nobody measures. Corrected in `seo-technical-auditor` (both copies, 3 places + a dated Core-Web-Vitals note), `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/seo-growth/SKILL.md`, and `strategy/playbooks/phase-0-discovery.md` + its dual-located twin. **(b) Retired tooling.** Rule 6 sent auditors to the **Mobile-Friendly Test**, which Google sunset along with its API and the Mobile Usability report on **2023-12-01** — now the URL Inspection tool's rendered-HTML view, which is the instrument the rule was actually reaching for; and "GSC Index Coverage report" → its current name, the **Page indexing** report.

**Attribution/licensing:** ideas-only, written from scratch; surfacing credited in-file to kostja94/marketing-skills (MIT, license re-verified via the GitHub API this run). **Its 404-vs-410 distinction was deliberately NOT adopted as stated** — Google's status-code documentation says all `4xx` except `429` are treated the same, so that row was rebuilt from the primary source and `410` is presented as a signal to humans and other systems, never as a faster de-index lever. Ours: the four-disposition model, the exclusion-is-not-a-defect rule, the deferred-is-a-volume-question-before-a-server-question read, the route-don't-fix handoff, the gated-PDF `X-Robots-Tag` case, the intended-exclusion map, and unknown-never-rounds-to-indexed. Every state definition, directive behavior, and status-code claim quoted from and cited to Google primary docs read 2026-08-10 ([Page indexing report](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/7440203), [Block search indexing with noindex](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/block-indexing), [HTTP status codes and network errors](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/http-network-errors), [Remove information from Google](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/remove-information)); CWV dates/thresholds to [web.dev](https://web.dev/articles/inp) and the [INP launch post](https://web.dev/blog/inp-cwv-launch). **No indexation-rate, ranking, or recovery-time figure is asserted.**

**Verified:** lint **2/2** on both copies; copies **diff-identical**; `phase-0-discovery` copies diff-identical; 0 broken internal `.md` links (286 checked, re-run after edits); 8 of the 9 new external URLs HTTP 200 — the ninth (Search Engine Land, cited only for the Mobile-Friendly Test sunset *date*) 403s to `curl` under bot protection but was fetched live this run and confirms the date while quoting Google's own announcement.

**Deferred:** the `seo-programmatic-strategist` proposal (maintainer scope decision); all blocked P1 items unchanged.

The one remaining unblocked P2 curation slice — `analytics-and-reporting`, the last of the four `social-media-skills/skills` slices — was **investigated and declined as a non-gap**: the vanity-vs-actionable-metric discipline is already repo-wide (present in *every* `social-*` agent), and the goal→metric→report-structure discipline is squarely owned by `analytics-performance-analyst` and `ops-reporting-specialist` (both carry the "report for decisions, not data exhaustiveness" thesis); the source is creator/DTC-shaped. A social-specific reporting section would duplicate two existing owners. So the backlog was quiet → freshness pass (ROUTINE step 3).

**What genuinely changed.** Google shipped attribution changes to AI Overviews / AI Mode on **2026-05-06** ([blog.google, "How AI Mode and AI Overviews help you explore the web"](https://blog.google/products-and-platforms/products/search/explore-web-generative-ai-search/), read 2026-08-09, verified against [Search Engine Land 476571](https://searchengineland.com/google-updates-links-within-ai-overviews-ai-mode-476571)) that the 2026-07-28 review missed. Two are directly material and were absent repo-wide: (1) **inline citations now sit right next to the specific sentence they support** — "you'll now also see more links directly within AI responses — right next to the relevant text" — which raises the payoff of a clean, self-contained, extractable passage (reinforces §3); and (2) **when the cited source is a discussion/social post, the link shows the creator's name/handle and the community name, not just the domain** — "adding more context to these links, like a creator's name, handle, or community name." That gives the existing **Reddit ≈ 6× G2** signal (§2/§4/checklist #12) a concrete mechanism: the account you post under and the subreddit are surfaced to the buyer, so authentic participation under a real identity beats an anonymous brand drop. The three weaker UX features in the same announcement (hover previews, next-step suggestions, subscription highlighting) were deliberately **not** added — low actionability for a citation playbook.

**Not a GEO effect-size change.** Cross-checked the KDD 2024 GEO paper: the "+27.8% quotes / +25.9% stats / +24.9% cite-sources" figures circulating are the paper's *average* metric, not a new study — our playbook already cites the same paper's Position-Adjusted-Word-Count headline numbers (+40 / +33 / +28). No effect size touched.

**Verify before claiming done:** `scripts/lint-agents.sh` on both edited agent copies — **2/2 pass**; the two copies are **diff-identical**; repo-wide internal `.md` link check re-run — **277 checked, 0 broken**; both manifests re-validated with `jq`. **Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration.

**Run shape — recovery, not a fresh scan.** This run opened on a **dirty working tree**: an earlier skill-scout run today had written a complete, coherent enhancement to both dual-located copies of `pmm-launch-manager.md` and was interrupted before committing or recording anything (no 08-09 entry in this log, no ledger row, `git log` still at 08-08). Rather than discard finished work or stack a second change on top of it, this run **verified, completed, and recorded that change** — which is also why the survey below is deliberately narrow. One change per run still holds.

**Verified before trusting the inherited work** (none of it taken on faith from the interrupted run): the source repo is live and **MIT** (confirmed via the GitHub license API, not just its LICENSE text); `pmm-execution/skills/stakeholder-maps/SKILL.md` exists and was read in full — it does build political maps of "who can kill your launch," with champion/blocker roles and an explicit *silent blocker scan*, so the attribution's characterization is accurate rather than assumed; and both seam claims in the new prose check out against our own files — `sales-deal-strategist` maps the **customer's** committee (economic buyer, blocker, champion) and `pm-campaign-coordinator`'s stakeholder map is a *communications* plan (role, frequency, method, escalation path), neither of which reads an internal stakeholder's stance toward the launch.

### 2026-08-08 — Launch Manager learns to assume the launch already failed (automated skill-scout run)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration. Market intelligence + adoption, distinct from the [ROUTINE.md](ROUTINE.md) maintenance run that also ran today.

**Focus discipline (by rotation):** PMM / sales / GTM — last the focus discipline 2026-08-04, the oldest of the four (email/analytics/ops 08-05, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-06, paid/social 08-07). Its curation queue entered the run empty; the only open PMM item is the pricing-owner SCOPE QUESTION, which needs a maintainer decision rather than more evidence.

**Surveyed:** 16 sources, all recorded in [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md) with verdicts — 1 enhance (+1 co-credit), 2 watch, 12 dismissed.

**Shipped:** **Critical Rule 9**, a new **"Assume It Already Failed: The Launch Pre-Mortem"** section, a **Launch Pre-Mortem & Risk Register** deliverable, and two success metrics, in both dual-located copies of `pmm-launch-manager.md` — **the first enhancement this agent has ever received** (67 → 103 lines).

**The gap sat inside the agent's own risk apparatus.** Rules 2, 3, and 4 are a complete system for *schedule and completion* risk — a 30% timeline buffer, a signed readiness checklist, weekly dependency reviews. All three ask whether the work is done and on time; **none asks whether the launch will work**, and the file's single postponement trigger (Rule 3) fires on an unchecked box. Grep-verified before writing: `pre-mortem`, `premortem`, `prospective hindsight`, `red team`, `falsif`, and `tripwire` returned **zero** hits repo-wide; `risk register` existed only in `pm-client-success-manager` (agency delivery risk) and `ops-legal-compliance` (compliance risk); `kill criteri` only in `paid-media-budget-optimizer` (spend); `postpone` **only** in this agent's own Rule 3.

**Sources & what is ours.** Ideas-only, written from scratch. The technique is Gary Klein's ([Performing a Project Premortem](https://hbr.org/2007/09/performing-a-project-premortem), HBR September 2007, fetched and verified this run); its structuring as a marketing deliverable — three-way disposition, owner-plus-observable per blocking risk, go/conditional/hold verdict — was learned from `pre-mortem` in [stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills](https://github.com/stefanoskarakasis/Product-Marketing-Skills) (MIT, verified live) and independently corroborated by `meta/orchestration/premortem` in [matteotitta/genesys-skills](https://github.com/matteotitta/genesys-skills) (MIT, co-credited). **Six layers are ours:** the before-or-after-launch split on every observable (pre-launch-testable risks are unfinished *tasks*, post-launch-only ones are *tripwires* with responses written in advance, and the two counts are reported separately); the run-before-sign-off ordering rule and the don't-facilitate-your-own-date rule; the one-sales-cycle horizon in place of a generic three months; expiring acceptance (every accepted trade-off records the condition it was accepted under, re-read at the next launch); the instrument routing table pointing six standard failure narratives at the agents that already own the test (messaging 08-05, ad-library scan 08-07, proof points, advocacy, the two analytics agents); and the zero-blocking-risks-is-a-failed-exercise rule with a calibration log that records **which real failures nobody named**.

**Honesty note.** The widely-repeated "prospective hindsight improves identification of future-outcome causes by ~30%" figure is **deliberately not asserted** — Mitchell, Russo & Pennington (1989) report that outcome *uncertainty* strongly shaped explanations while temporal perspective did little, so the popular number is contested against its own source. The file says so in the open and rests the technique on what is defensible. No launch-outcome or risk-detection figure is asserted anywhere in the section.

**One metric fixed in passing** (same call as 07-30, 08-05, 08-06, 08-07): "Launch Readiness Score — 100% of function teams sign off … zero 'not ready' items" is precisely the metric the new section says cannot bear the weight put on it, and is now labeled a *completion* measure to be paired with the two new pre-mortem metrics rather than read as confidence.

**Verify before claiming done:** `scripts/lint-agents.sh` on both edited copies — **2/2 pass**; the two copies are **diff-identical**; repo-wide internal link check re-run after the edit — **268 checked, 0 broken**; of the four new external URLs, three return HTTP 200 and the fourth (the DOI) was confirmed resolving 302 → Wiley Online Library, whose 403 to bots is not a broken link.

**Deferred:** `retro` from the same source (Rule 8 ends with "conduct post-launch retrospective within 2 weeks" and supplies no method — and today's section just handed that retrospective a specific job), `stakeholder-maps` (internal approvers/blockers; check overlap with `pm-campaign-coordinator`), the `msdanyg/pmm-impact-audit-skill` function-assessment scope question, and `cagatayuncu/marketing-machine`'s claim-provenance primitive — all filed to the backlog. **The gap is dead-center in a discipline the agent already claims.** Rule 2 commits it to "test 3–5 thread variations monthly to identify highest-engagement formats," and its deliverables promise "thread templates with actual engagement data showing which variations perform best" — but the file supplied **no method**: no single-variable isolation, no decision rule, no acknowledgement that organic social can't reach statistical significance, and Success Metrics ("20-30% of weekly tweets generate 3x+ average engagement") that read a winner straight off single-post performance. Grep-verified before writing: `paired repetition`, `decision rule`, `holdout`, `underpowered`, and `minimum detectable` returned **zero** hits across `social/` and the plugin social dir.

The seam is the same certify-vs-verdict shape used for the 2026-08-05 message-validation and 2026-08-03 CPL reconciliations: `paid-media-creative-strategist` owns the *powered* paid test (a budget lever buys sample → compute an MDE, four-outcome verdict, "when you cannot power it, do not fake it"); this owns the *structurally-unpowerable* organic case (no budget lever, small samples, opaque algorithmic delivery), where rigor is **one variable + a decision rule fixed before publishing + matched controls over 1–4 weeks + 3–5 paired repetitions + ties logged as ties**. The two agree on what a winner is — neither promotes a single favourable read — and a format proven organically is re-tested under the paid discipline if budget is later put behind it. Where a real concurrent control exists (YouTube Test & Compare) the section says to use it instead of sequential guessing.

**Sources & honesty:** ideas-only, written from scratch; the "controls-and-repetition instead of p-values" spine adapted from the `experimentation-and-ab-testing` skill in [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) (MIT, Copyright 2026 Frank Heijdenrijk, verified this run). The ~20% materiality threshold is flagged **directional** and explicitly self-calibrated to an account's own week-to-week variance, **not** a benchmark; **no engagement or lift figure is asserted**; the organic-vs-powered seam, the calibrate-to-your-own-variance rule, and the metric are ours.

### 2026-08-07 — Programmatic Media Buyer learns that the impression log, not the buy, is what it purchased (automated **skill-scout** run)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration. **Focus discipline: paid media / social**, chosen by rotation — it was last the focus on 2026-08-03, the oldest of the four (PMM/sales/GTM 08-04, email/analytics/ops 08-05, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-06), and its queue entered this run empty (CPL-vs-CRM reconciliation closed, the stale Meta Audience Network line in `phase-4-launch` fixed, both halves of the `ad-mastermind` mine shipped 08-06 and 08-07).

**Scouted:** 13 sources → 1 enhance, 1 watch, 11 dismissed. Full rows in [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**Shipped:** a new **"Buying an Audience, Receiving an Impression: Auditing Supply Quality"** section, **Critical Rule 9**, and a **Supply Quality & Domain Disposition Audit** deliverable, in both dual-located copies of `paid-media-programmatic-buyer.md` — **the first enhancement this agent has ever received** (61 lines, untouched since the first pass; it was one of only two paid agents in that state).

**This run took a gap, not a source's idea, and the absence is the finding.** Every tracked paid collection was re-checked *by file tree* for buy-side supply coverage — `claude-ads`, `ad-superpowers-plugin`, `gmmh1/claude-paid-media-skills`, `hyperfx-ai/marketing-skills`, `rama-marketing`, plus the 224★ `ivangfalco/ads-skills`. Their entire display surface is three files, all Google Display Network *campaign setup* (responsive display ads, placement targeting as a reach lever, audience lists). **Not one mentions `ads.txt`, `sellers.json`, supply paths, MFA, or the measurable/viewable distinction.** The single source with any invalid-traffic content at all — `virajsutar-marketer/paid-ads-agent`, new and MIT — has four lines of search-side click-pattern watching.

Grep-verified the gap before writing: **`ads.txt`, `sellers.json`, `supply chain`, `supply path`, `Active View`, `measurable impression`, `log-level`, `domain report`, `GIVT`, `SIVT`, `pre-bid`, `verification vendor`, `arbitrage`, `inclusion list`, `invalid traffic`, `click fraud`, `MFA site`, and `made for advertising` returned zero hits repo-wide.** `viewab` returned exactly one — a blurb line in `paid-media-ops/SKILL.md`, in no agent. `premium publisher` and `allowlist` returned hits only inside this agent's own Rule 6 and its Brand Safety deliverable: the control asserted, the instrument absent.

**The organizing idea is the display-layer form of a thesis this repo has now applied three times** (LinkedIn delivery-vs-targeting 08-03, anomaly instrument-first 08-05): the buy is a *request for an audience*; what arrives is a *log of impressions on domains*, sold through a chain of intermediaries, some share of which was never measurable.

The section supplies the **three-questions split** (brand safety, invalid traffic, and supply quality are three questions with three instruments — Rule 6 collapsed them into one line and left the third with none); the finding that **the fraud number is someone else's log**, since Google filters IVT pre-bid and credits it post-serve and the "Invalid clicks" column reports traffic *already detected and already not charged*; the **B2B cheap-CPM inversion** (a small budget against a narrow audience pressures the DSP to find *any* matching inventory, and MFA inventory matches every segment because its audience was purchased — so a surprisingly cheap CPM on a hard-to-reach audience is a diagnostic, not a win); the **measurable/viewable pairing rule**, because viewable rate is denominated in *measurable* impressions rather than delivered ones, so a placement you mostly cannot see into reports a flattering rate on the sliver you can; the **spend-sorted domain report** with four dispositions and a **concentration-curve read** (thousands of domains each taking a sliver is not reach — it is the DSP settling for whatever cleared the bid); the three public supply-chain standards scoped to the two questions a buyer can actually answer; and the **exclusion treadmill and its inversion** — MFA supply regenerates faster than a block list grows, so the structural fix is an inclusion list, which costs reach and raises CPMs, and that is the correct B2B trade because reach was never the binding constraint.

**Standing disciplines applied again:** *unmeasurable never rounds to viewable, it rounds to unknown* (its own disposition row, never merged into performing/not-performing); and **log-level data access is a contract term, not a setting**, so where the agreement does not grant it, supply-path quality is reported as *unverified* rather than as a passing control.

**Seams drawn explicitly:** Google Ads placement exclusions and their documented limits are the PPC Strategist's ground (its negative-keyword section already carries the 1,000-negatives-on-Display-and-Video limit) — maintain **one** shared exclusion list, not two that drift. And supply quality is a *precondition*, not a result: a clean audit on a campaign with no incrementality evidence is a well-run buy of **unknown value**, which hands the question back to Rule 4.

**Two unmeasurable success metrics fixed in passing** (the same call made for `paid-media-creative-strategist` 07-30, `analytics-performance-analyst` 08-05, and `seo-ai-search-optimizer` 08-06): "Maintain **<2% fraudulent impression rate**" measured the platform's own filter rather than the buy, and "Achieve **85%+ of impressions on premium publishers**" set a percentage against a publisher tier the file never defines. Both replaced — the first with disposition coverage plus an explicit instruction *not* to report the platform's invalid-traffic column as an achievement, the second with the measurable-rate/viewable-rate pair and inclusion-list spend share measured against the account's own baseline.

**No MFA waste percentage is asserted.** Published estimates vary widely by study, year, and SSP, and the ANA's own follow-up work reported a large decline once buyers began excluding it — so the file instructs measuring your own domain report rather than importing anyone's figure. Every fact is quoted from and cited to a primary source read 2026-08-07: [DV360 — Filtering invalid traffic](https://support.google.com/displayvideo/answer/6076504), [Google Ads — About invalid traffic](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/11182074), [IAB Tech Lab — Using OpenRTB Signals to Identify MFA](https://iabtechlab.com/using-openrtb-signals-to-identify-made-for-advertising/), [Google Ads — Active View / viewability](https://support.google.com/google-ads/answer/7029393), [IAB Tech Lab — ads.txt](https://iabtechlab.com/ads-txt/), [IAB Tech Lab — sellers.json & SupplyChain Object](https://iabtechlab.com/sellers-json/).

**Verified:** `bash scripts/lint-agents.sh` on both copies → **2/2 pass**; `diff` → copies identical; all 6 new external URLs return HTTP 200.

**Deferred:** [social-media-skills/skills](https://github.com/social-media-skills/skills) (14★, 106 skills, MIT) filed to backlog — no paid coverage at all, mostly creator/DTC-shaped, but its `analytics-and-reporting`, `experimentation-and-ab-testing`, `crisis-and-moderation`, and `linkedin-company-pages` slices are worth a read on a future social run, and `social-linkedin-strategist` (85 lines) has never been enhanced. `ivangfalco/ads-skills` (224★) noted as unlicensed/ideas-only with `audience-sizing` as the one thing worth a later look.

Still live + MIT; still 0★, but now carrying **documented field-scan runs (170 Meta ads) and completed gauntlet runs that log where their own blinding failed** (`blinding_failed` in their scorecard) instead of hiding it — the "seen to survive contact" bar the 2026-08-03 note set, met by honest limitation-reporting rather than by stars (which this repo's philosophy has never treated as evidence). **Shipped:** a new **"The Discrimination Control: Proving the Scorer Actually Discriminated"** section in both dual-located copies of `ops-quality-assurance.md`. The Reference Class section (2026-07-31) names "**a generous scorer is useless**" as its operative discipline but has no way to *detect* one — a reviewer, or an AI-assisted scoring pass, can grade a whole set of variants uniformly **strong** and the copy-scoring pass had no catch. Gap grep-verified before writing: `decoy` / `negative anchor` / `discrimination control` / `blind judg*` all **zero** repo-wide. The section: when scoring a *set* judged together, seed one deliberately weak **negative-anchor decoy**, judge blind with it unmarked, and **read the decoy's placement first** — if it isn't at/near the bottom the **round is invalid** and its scores are discarded, not salvaged; + an honest-decoy construction rule (not a joke line, not a strong line in disguise; a documented past under-performer when scoring against Tier-1 data), a log-pass-or-fail discipline mirroring the source's transparency, and a **decoy proves discrimination, not quality** guardrail (ratifies the ranking, never the winner; set-scoring only — a lone deliverable has no ranking to invalidate).

**Seam:** none needed — `ops-quality-assurance` owns copy scoring outright, so this sits beside its own Reference Class and Four U's sections on the same agent.

**Verified:** lint **2/2**; both copies **diff-identical**; 256-file internal-link scan re-run after the edit — still **zero broken** (the one link added is external).

**Ideas-only; no fabricated metrics.** The seed-a-decoy-and-void-the-round mechanic is credited in-file to [olson-adam/ad-mastermind](https://github.com/olson-adam/ad-mastermind) (MIT, re-verified 2026-08-06), which applies it to scoring ad concepts against real market campaigns; the set-size boundary, the honest-decoy construction rule, and the proves-discrimination-not-quality guardrail are ours. No detection-accuracy or scoring figures asserted.

The blocked PROPOSAL/SCOPE-QUESTION items remain blocked on maintainer decisions as recorded.

### 2026-08-06 — AI Search Optimizer learns to measure the traffic AI answers actually send (automated **maintenance** run)

**Job:** [ROUTINE.md](ROUTINE.md), one iteration.

**Backlog pick:** the highest-priority genuinely-unblocked item — the newest skill-curation entry, **AI-referral traffic measurement** (surfaced 2026-08-06 from [jstanx/aeo-toolkit](https://github.com/jstanx/aeo-toolkit), MIT). The one open P1 high-leverage item (native subagents) is explicitly blocked on maintainer decisions; the P1 distribution items are each gated (awesome-claude-code on subjective "README polished" judgment; VoltAgent on the no-fabricated-usage guardrail since there is no genuine usage to lead with yet; the Anthropic marketplace needs an in-app form I can't drive headlessly; the rest on a star threshold).

**Shipped:** a new **"Measuring Arrival: The Traffic AI Answers Actually Send"** section in both dual-located copies of `seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`, plus a one-sentence reciprocal cross-reference in both copies of `analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md` (GA4 audit, check 6).

The agent's own funnel is stated as `reachable → recognized → quotable` and its Success Metrics already promise to "attribute 5-10% of monthly qualified traffic to AI answer engine referrals" — a fourth stage (**arrival**) the agent committed to a number for but had no method for. Gap grep-verified before writing: **`chatgpt.com`, `utm_source=chatgpt`, and `AI referral` returned zero hits** in agent content (only the ledger/backlog); the `referrer` hits are newsletter mechanics and the `referral traffic` hits are all `seo-link-building-strategist` on link quality. The section's spine: a near-zero AI-referral number is usually a **measurement artifact, not a finding** — native assistant apps, copy-pasted links, and in-assistant browsers arrive with no document referrer and fall into GA4 **Direct** (definition quoted from Google, read 2026-08-06), so the observed number is a **floor, not a total**; UTM tagging works only on links you actually control; and because volume is understated you judge the segment by conversion/pipeline, never by raw session count.

**Seam decided in-file:** the section lives on the **AEO agent** (it completes that agent's own funnel and backs its own unbacked *Citation traffic attribution* metric), while the GA4 **construction** — the custom channel group, the referrer-domain match list, and the observed/modeled/missing decomposition — is handed to `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`, whose established measurement-layer craft it is; the Ops Architect gets a one-line pointer back. Interpretation on one agent, construction on the other, navigable from both sides — the same certify-vs-verdict shape used for the 2026-08-03 CPL reconciliation.

**Verified:** lint 4/4; both agent copies diff-identical; 174-file link scan still zero broken; both cross-reference section titles resolve in both directions.

**Ideas-only; no fabricated metrics.** The idea is credited to `jstanx/aeo-toolkit` (MIT); the floor-not-total framing, the controlled-links-only UTM rule, the judge-the-segment discipline, and the construction/interpretation seam are ours. **No figure is asserted for the share of AI traffic that arrives without a referrer** — it is unmeasured by nature, which is the section's whole point.

**Deferred:** nothing new surfaced this run (a maintenance run, not a scout run); the open PROPOSAL/SCOPE-QUESTION backlog items remain blocked on maintainer decisions as recorded.

### 2026-08-06 — AI Search Optimizer learns that recognition is settled off your own site (automated **skill-scout** run)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration. **Focus discipline: content / SEO-AEO-GEO**, chosen by rotation — it was last the focus on 2026-08-02, the oldest of the four (paid/social 08-03, PMM/sales/GTM 08-04, email/analytics/ops 08-05), and its queue entered this run empty (decay triage 08-02, cannibalization audit 08-02, crawler access 07-30, AEO↔Reddit seam 08-02 all closed).

**Scouted:** 17 sources → 1 enhance (+1 co-credit), 2 watch, 13 dismissed. Full rows in [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**Shipped:** a new **"Recognition Before Citation: The Entity Lives Off Your Site"** section, **Critical Rule 10**, and an **Entity Recognition Audit & Reconciliation Plan** deliverable, in both dual-located copies of `seo-ai-search-optimizer.md`.

Critical Rule 2 asserts that "AI systems heavily weight entity signals for citation decisions" — and then sends the reader entirely to their own JSON-LD; the Entity Markup Implementation Plan deliverable is on-site only. Markup is a *claim you make about yourself*; recognition is a *conclusion the engine reaches* from how consistently the rest of the web describes the same company. The agent owned only the half of the entity a company can write itself. Grep-verified the gap first: **`sameAs`, `knowledge graph`, `Wikidata`, `disambiguat`, `entity home`, `Person schema`, `notability`, `unlinked mention`, and `About page` returned zero hits repo-wide**, and `Organization schema` appeared in exactly two agents, both times as a markup-checklist line.

The section supplies `sameAs` as a one-place identity join rather than a link tactic (with the point that a set pointing at three disagreeing profiles has only made the inconsistency machine-readable); a B2B SaaS corroboration set diffed row by row against one canonical fact record; **fact drift as a dated-event problem** — the wrong description of a company is almost never invented, it is *the last true thing still being repeated* — so the sweep attaches to the raise / rename / acquisition / leadership announcement (handed to `pmm-launch-manager`) rather than to a hygiene calendar; disambiguation for name collisions and the two-entities-in-the-wild period after a rebrand; **Wikidata and Wikipedia separated as different bars with different risks**; the knowledge panel read as an instrument you can claim and suggest edits to, never author; and a recognition test built on **brand questions graded on the description**, distinct from the topic-question citability heatmap shipped 07-26.

**The seam was drawn explicitly:** `pmm-customer-advocacy` owns whether the reviews are good and plentiful; this owns whether the profile describes the same company the homepage does.

**A hard guardrail that neither source has:** the Wikimedia Terms of Use require paid contributors to disclose employer, client, and affiliation, and the COI guideline directs them to talk pages or Articles for Creation — so "get a Wikipedia page" is not a marketing task to assign, and an undisclosed agency-written article is a Terms-of-Use violation whose public record is itself citable, a materially worse outcome than no article. The agent is now instructed never to recommend it.

**Two unmeasurable success metrics fixed in passing** (the same call made for `paid-media-creative-strategist` 07-30 and `analytics-performance-analyst` 08-05): "Improve **Entity Authority scores** (measured through entity extract APIs) by 30-40%" names a score no entity-extraction API publishes, and "achieve **top 3 entity rankings** across 5-10 core topics" names a ranking that does not exist. Both were replaced with measurable statements — fact consistency against the canonical record, re-verified on announcement events rather than on a calendar; topic coverage read off the share-of-voice heatmap *with its sample size* — and a brand-question recognition metric added, in which *recognized-and-wrong* reaches zero before *unknown* is worked.

**Sources verified:** `entity-optimization` in [jstanx/aeo-toolkit](https://github.com/jstanx/aeo-toolkit) (1★, pushed 2026-06-14), **MIT confirmed via the GitHub license API**, co-credited with an independent second sighting in [Thibaultbm/claude-seo-geo](https://github.com/Thibaultbm/claude-seo-geo) (13★, MIT, pushed 2026-08-03), whose `geo-visibility` skill defines an entity as brand identity models learn "from co-occurrence across the web." Ideas only, written from scratch. **Ours:** the dated-event drift model, the recognized-and-wrong triage order, the brand-question-vs-topic-question split, the Wikipedia paid-editing guardrail, the advocacy seam, and the explicit no-transfer-function note (no engine documents how it resolves contradictory entity facts, so consistency is risk reduction under uncertainty, never a promised lift).

**No citation or ranking effect asserted.** Every fact is cited to a primary source read 2026-08-06: [schema.org/sameAs](https://schema.org/sameAs), [Google Organization structured data](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/appearance/structured-data/organization), [Google knowledge panel help](https://support.google.com/knowledgepanel/answer/9163198), [Wikidata:Notability](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Wikidata:Notability), [Wikipedia:Paid-contribution disclosure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Paid-contribution_disclosure).

**Verified:** `bash scripts/lint-agents.sh` on both copies → **2/2 pass**; `diff` → copies identical; both cross-referenced agent files (`pmm-customer-advocacy`, `pmm-launch-manager`) exist on disk; all 7 new external URLs return HTTP 200.

**Deferred:** the same repo's `ai-referral-analytics` — a real gap (nothing in the repo measures the traffic answer engines actually send, and much of it lands in Direct because no referrer is passed) — filed to backlog with the Ops-Architect-vs-AEO seam to decide first. Index-bloat scoring and a page-type taxonomy were recorded against the open `seo-programmatic-strategist` proposal rather than actioned.

**Shipped:** a new **"Messaging by Segment: Route the Entry Point, Don't Fork the House"** section plus **Critical Rule 10**, a **Segment Entry-Point Routing Map** deliverable, a **`Segment Routing Integrity`** success metric, and a pointer added to Rule 3 — in both dual-located copies of `pmm-messaging-architect.md`.

The gap sat inside the agent's own Rule 3, which names **two** variation axes (persona, buying moment) and omits **market segment** — the axis sales asks for constantly. Grep-verified before writing: `vertical messaging`, `industry messaging`, and `swap test` returned zero hits repo-wide; every `vertical` hit was an ICP *targeting* dimension or a reference-account *tag*, and the only messaging mention was one clause inside a deliverable ("industry/vertical pages").

The adopted idea is the source's: segment messaging is a **routing** problem, not an authoring one — lock the library, vary only which pillar leads and the one-to-two-line entry point, route by **buying trigger** rather than standing pain, never invent a trigger, and apply the **swap test**. **Six layers are ours:** (a) the two failure modes and their maintenance cost — an averaged message that describes no one, versus a per-vertical fork that makes every repositioning an N-times job and hands the 07-31 consistency audit N× the pairs; (b) an **axis-recognizability test** — one axis per map, and if a buyer wouldn't use it to describe themselves unprompted it is a CRM reporting dimension; (c) **trigger perishability** — a passed compliance deadline reads exactly as persuasive as it did when true, so every trigger carries a date and a review class (deadline / wave / structural) and the map is re-verified quarterly, not semi-annually; (d) the **base-rate counterfactual** — the segment's non-buyers lived through the same regulation and audit season, so a trigger earns its row only if it appears disproportionately in accounts that entered a cycle, read against win/loss with `pmm-competitive-intelligence`; (e) the **segment-proof credibility check** — naming a vertical promises vertical evidence, and `pmm-customer-advocacy` already tags references by vertical/industry, so an unbacked row softens to a segment-*framed* claim or waits with the missing reference logged; (f) the **merge test** — two segments routing to the same pillar with interchangeable entry points are one segment.

**Not adopted:** the source's numeric confidence thresholds (≥60% verified / ≤10% estimated rows) — an arbitrary percentage bar a three-segment map cannot meaningfully meet; replaced with a per-row, per-surface rule under the repo's standing *inferred never rounds to verified* discipline.

**Deferred to backlog:** (1) `Fearofsnakes/pmm-skillset`'s `message-market-fit` — a *method* for Rule 5's un-methodised "test messaging before rollout," including a pre-test feasibility gate and a composite scorecard of mixed quantitative and qualitative signals; the seam with `paid-media-creative-strategist`'s pre-registration discipline (07-30) needs deciding first. (2) The **pricing/packaging scope question** is escalated from "recorded" to "decide" — this run found the third and fourth independent sightings (`taizen-ai`, `genesys-skills`), after `firatcand` and `TerminalSkills` on 07-31.

**Verified:** `bash scripts/lint-agents.sh` → 2/2 passed; both copies diff-identical; no new external links beyond the two GitHub source URLs.

**License posture:** ideas only, written from scratch in our voice; `matteotitta/genesys-skills` is MIT and is credited in-file with our own layers marked as ours. No prose reused. No fabricated metrics — the section asserts no figures at all.

What was missing was not a *place* to store the list but the *discipline* that it is one cross-channel record read before every contact — and discipline is advisory, so the owner is the **Legal Compliance Officer**, not the channel agents and not a new shared file. Single owner, no channel-agent sprawl.

**Shipped (both dual-located copies of `client-ops/ops-legal-compliance.md`):** **Critical Rule 9** ("Treat Consent and Suppression as One Cross-Channel Record, Not Per-Tool State"); a **"Consent Is One Record; Every Channel Reads It Before Contact"** section (per-tool consent fragmentation; GDPR Art. 21 / Art. 7(3) and CCPA/CPRA opt-out bind the *business*, not the tool; the CRM/CDP-not-repo-artifact placement; ad-platform custom/matched-audience uploads as a CPRA "share"/"sale" that must be scrubbed *before* upload and re-scrubbed on each refresh because matched audiences decay, with the service-provider-vs-third-party caveat handed to counsel; the deliverability specialist's line-140 sync raised from hygiene to consent obligation and extended past email to ad + sales channels); and a **Cross-Channel Suppression Coverage** success metric.

**Guardrails:** ideas-only, written in our voice; regulation cited to the primary statutes (GDPR Arts. 21/7(3), CCPA/CPRA sale-and-share), **no fabricated figures**, the CPRA ad-audience reading appropriately hedged for counsel; not personalized legal advice (same advisory register as the file's existing 8 rules); no personal/sensitive data.

- **Two-Views Model (v1 unbiased vs. v2 client-validated) — declined.** It presupposes their 12-Part per-engagement deliverable lifecycle: unbiased research docs produced in Parts 2–4, a formal Part 5 "client validation stop" (ACCEPT/REJECT/EDIT/DEFER on each finding), and selective v2 re-runs governed by a decision matrix, with both versioned views retained forever. This repo deliberately has none of that scaffolding — it is an advisory persona library + one shared `brand-context.md` + stateful loops, not an engagement-document framework. No change; recorded so a future run doesn't re-open it.
- **Living Instruction File — cache/auto-update machinery declined, the correction-propagation kernel adopted.** The LIF's core (a single "currently true," read-first, single-source-of-truth file every skill consults) *is* our `brand-context.md`; a second cached layer with versioned-source auto-update triggers would only duplicate it. But its **Update-Back Rule** exposed a real, small gap: `brand-context.md` was read-only-forever, carrying only a vague "review whenever positioning/pricing/ICP changes" footer and **no discipline for a correction discovered mid-work** — even though our *loops* already write corrections back (the ABM ledger, the intel snapshot). Grep-verified before writing: `write.?back / update.?back / propagat / correction` returned **zero hits** in either copy of the template, and no agent anywhere owns "correct brand-context when a fact proves wrong."

**Shipped:** `templates/brand-context.md` (both dual-located copies) — a new **Rule 6** in "Rules for agents reading this file" (when you or the user find a fact here is outdated or a cited proof point no longer holds — a churned reference customer, a withdrawn logo, a revised metric, a persona pain contradicted by field data — surface it with a `[CORRECT BRAND CONTEXT: …]` marker, reusing the existing `[NEEDS INPUT: …]` convention, rather than fixing it only in the one deliverable that caught it) plus a compact **§14 "Corrections & learnings"** dated table whose stated first job is withdrawn proof: a standing record that a customer, logo, or number must now *stop* appearing in public copy. This closes the evidence-integrity loop the file already half-owns (Rules 1–2 forbid inventing proof; nothing let it *retire* proof).

**Sourcing:** ideas only, written from scratch in the template's voice; no prose reused. Source MIT, credited here. No fabricated metrics; the one example table row is an angle-bracketed placeholder, consistent with the rest of the template.

### 2026-08-02 — Content Optimizer learns to diagnose a decline before prescribing a refresh (automated skill scout)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration. Focus discipline **content / SEO-AEO-GEO** by rotation — it was last the focus on 2026-07-30 (the second of that day's three runs, ahead of paid/social), while PMM/sales/GTM ran 07-31 and email/analytics/ops ran 08-01. 9 sources evaluated → 1 enhance (2 co-credits), 2 watch, 6 dismissed; every row appended to [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**Scouted:** the find is [AgriciDaniel/claude-blog](https://github.com/AgriciDaniel/claude-blog) (MIT, 1,543★, pushed 2026-07-23) — the fourth repo from this author to be surfaced but the **first time this one has been opened** (claude-ads was mined 07-21/07-26/07-30, claude-seo 07-21). Its `blog-decay` skill treats content decay as a period-over-period comparison whose triage branch is *allowed not to end in an edit*: refresh / investigate query shift / consolidate-redirect / prune, with indexation, canonicals, backlinks, and seasonality validated before any action. The same discipline turned up independently in two other collections this run ([rampstackco/claude-skills](https://github.com/rampstackco/claude-skills), MIT — "refresh-vs-merge-vs-delete decisions"; [inhouseseo/superseo-skills](https://github.com/inhouseseo/superseo-skills), Apache-2.0 — content decay as a *nightly signal* rather than a quarterly sweep), which is what moved it from interesting to worth acting on. Notably, **none of the five previously-mined SEO/AEO sources owns it either** — `claude-seo`'s 33 skills cover audit, cluster, content-brief, drift, geo, programmatic and more, and nothing there touches the decay/prune lifecycle.

**Gap verified before writing (required step):** grepped repo-wide — `content decay`, `dropped out`, `deindex`, `content inventory`, and `historical optimization` returned **zero hits each**; `redirect` and `canonical` existed only in `seo-technical-auditor` (technical SEO, not content lifecycle); `retire` only in the analytics agents, meaning *field* and *campaign* retirement. That left `seo-content-optimizer` — an agent whose Identity asserts that "80% of SEO wins come from strategic improvements to existing content" — with two holes inside its own logic. Its trigger is a **calendar** (Rule 7: quarterly review of the top 100 pages), which spends equal hours on pages that did not move and never reaches page 140 where the collapse actually happened. And **every branch of its output ends in an edit**: consolidation was one clause inside one deliverable, and pruning was absent entirely.

**Shipped:** a new **"Reading a Decline Before Prescribing a Fix"** section plus **Critical Rule 9**, a **Content Decay Triage** deliverable, and two success metrics (`Decay diagnosis coverage`, `Disposition mix`), in both dual-located copies. Rule 7 gains a pointer clarifying that the calendar sets the *review cadence*, not the work queue.

**The sharpest parts are ours, not the source's.** (a) A **click floor applied before any percentage**: a 60% decline on a page that had 12 clicks last quarter is seven clicks — one procurement cycle, one changed internal link, one analyst's research habit — and B2B SaaS libraries are full of pages at those volumes, so a decay report ranked by percentage change is topped almost entirely by noise. The floor is stated in the report along with how many pages fell beneath it, rather than pages being silently dropped. This is the same *a percentage computed over too few events is not a weak signal, it is not a signal* discipline shipped to `paid-media-creative-strategist` on 07-30, and it is why the source's own 20/40/60% severity tiers were **not** adopted. (b) A **four-signature diagnosis grid** reading impressions and position together, since falling clicks is only the symptom: demand loss (impressions down, position stable), ranking loss (impressions stable, position down), **SERP interception** (both stable, clicks gone), and the intent re-alignment that looks like damage but is often the intended result. (c) The **interception row** and its inference boundary — the row that punishes a reflexive refresh and the one that became common in 2026. The page is winning the ranking and losing the click, so "make it more thorough and better structured" produces a *more liftable* answer and can make the outcome worse; that page routes to `seo-ai-search-optimizer`, whose job is being the cited source rather than the clicked one. (d) The **site-wide-cause check** — if the whole property fell by roughly the same amount, the page is not the story, and a page-level fix applied to a site-level cause burns the hours *and* hides the real cause. (e) The **asymmetry guardrail** — merging is a morning's work and unmerging is a quarter's, a redirect is cheap to add and expensive to unwind three redirects later, so anything that removes a URL inherits the repo's existing spend-change discipline: baseline before, batches small enough to attribute, a held rollback, and measurement of **both** directions, including what the removed pages were quietly still contributing.

**Also ours: "leave alone" as an output.** Three of the four dispositions are not a refresh. Retirement is decided by *what points at the URL* — external links, internal links, live campaigns, sales collateral — never by word count, and it resolves into three different actions (redirect / noindex-and-keep / delete). A documented no-action verdict carries its date so the next audit does not re-litigate it.

**Sourcing:** ideas only, written from scratch in our voice; no prose reused. The one factual block is Google's Search Console **generative AI performance report** — it covers AI Overviews and AI Mode, reports **impressions only** (no clicks, no position, no queries), is rolling out to a subset of properties, and its data is a subset of what already sits inside the Web search type of the main Performance report, so totals do not change when access arrives. That yields the section's inference boundary: AI-surface *exposure* is confirmable, AI-surface *click loss* is not — "shown inside AI features while position held and clicks fell" is permitted, "AI Overviews took N clicks" is forbidden. Cited to [Search Console Help, Generative AI performance report (Search)](https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/16984139), read 2026-08-02, with an explicit re-check note since metrics and availability change. No other figures asserted anywhere in the section; both success metrics are process checks or self-baselined ("baseline from your own first three cycles"), not benchmarks.

**Verify:** `scripts/lint-agents.sh` on both edited copies — **2/2 pass** (2,245 words each); `diff` confirms the browseable and plugin copies are byte-identical; all four external URLs added return HTTP 200; no relative links were introduced.

**Deferred:** the same repo's `blog-cannibalization` skill is the natural follow-on and a real partial gap — we prevent cannibalization *prospectively* (`content-blog-strategist` Rule 8) and now resolve it *reactively* as today's Consolidate disposition, but nothing owns **detection on an existing library** (clustering pages by normalized keyword/stem/intent overlap, then grading severity by overlap type × ranking disparity into merge / canonical / differentiate / no-action). Filed to backlog. Also noted for a future content run: its `cognitive-load` reference, which has no analogue anywhere in our repo.

### 2026-08-01 — Marketing Ops Architect learns that consent decides which numbers are real (automated skill scout)

**Job:** [SKILL_SCOUT.md](SKILL_SCOUT.md), one iteration. Focus discipline **email / analytics / marketing-ops** by rotation — it was last the focus on 2026-07-30 (the earliest of that day's three runs) and PMM/sales/GTM ran 07-31. 8 sources evaluated → 1 enhance, 1 watch, 6 dismissed; all rows appended to [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**Shipped:** a new **"Consent Before Collection: Separating Observed Data from Modeled and Missing"** section in both dual-located copies of `analytics-marketing-ops-architect`, plus **Critical Rule 9** and a `Consent Coverage Transparency` success metric.

The gap was verified by grep before a word was written: **`Consent Mode` returned zero hits repo-wide**, as did `modeled conversion` and `tag governance`; `Tag Manager` appeared only as a setup-checklist line in two strategy playbooks; and the single mention of consent anywhere in the analytics stack was a half-clause naming "consent-mode gaps" as one possible cause of GA4 *Unassigned* traffic. That left the six-check GA4 instrumentation audit shipped 2026-07-27 resting on an unaudited assumption — that the data **arrived**. Consent is the one failure in the measurement layer that is invisible by construction: a broken UTM leaves an *Unassigned* row and a mis-scoped dimension leaves an `(other)` bucket, but a consent gap leaves no error, no row, and no warning — just a smaller, correctly-formatted number.

**The sharpest finding is ours, not the source's.** GA4 applies behavioral modeling only to properties collecting ≥1,000 events/day with `analytics_storage='denied'` for 7 consecutive days **and** ≥1,000 daily users with consent granted for 7 of the previous 28 — and Google is explicit that clearing both bars still guarantees nothing. Those are consumer-site volumes. A B2B SaaS company with a named-account motion is routinely one to two orders of magnitude below them, so the honest default hypothesis for our ICP is that a team has implemented advanced consent mode, is dutifully sending cookieless pings, and is having **none** of it modeled back into reports — paying advanced's implementation cost for basic's data coverage. Labeled in-file as our reasoning applied to Google's published requirements, not a figure Google publishes.

Also ours: the **observed / modeled / missing** three-state decomposition (with the rule that *unknown never rounds to observed*, one level deeper than the GA4 audit's *unknown never rounds to pass*), and three named failure modes — the **banner-change confound** (a CMP swap changes the instrument mid-series, invalidating every comparison spanning it), **ratios degrading worse than counts** (numerator and denominator are suppressed by different amounts and the errors do not cancel), and **the global average hiding the regional hole** (a 3% global dip can be a 40% hole in one market). Closed with an explicit boundary: lawfulness of consent basis and banner design belong to `ops-legal-compliance` and to counsel; this agent owns only which numbers are real.

**Sources & licensing:** the *dimension* was surfaced by the `/gtm-audit` skill in [cognyai/claude-code-marketing-skills](https://github.com/cognyai/claude-code-marketing-skills), which names Consent Mode among its container checks. That skill is **paywalled** ($9/mo, their MCP servers) and therefore neither evaluable nor adoptable — the same situation as its `/ga4-audit` on 2026-07-25, handled the same way: dimension credited, no content adopted, section written from scratch. Every factual claim — the four consent parameters, basic-vs-advanced tag behavior, modeling thresholds, reporting-identity states and data-quality-icon labels, and the Customer Match EEA consent requirement — is cited to Google's own Tag Platform, Analytics Help, and Google Ads Help documentation, read 2026-08-01, with a re-verify note since thresholds and product policies change. One claim was deliberately **not** made: a blanket "the DMA requires consent mode v2" statement could not be pinned to a primary source this run, so the EEA point is scoped narrowly to what Google's Customer Match documentation actually says.

**Verified:** `scripts/lint-agents.sh` passes on both copies (2/2); `diff` confirms the browseable and plugin copies are byte-identical; repo-wide link check clean.

**Deferred:** [indranilbanerjee/digital-marketing-pro](https://github.com/indranilbanerjee/digital-marketing-pro) has grown from the small repo assessed on 07-21 to 671★/158 skills and was pushed today — its analytics skills are already covered by our inventory, but its *Two-Views Model* and *Living Project Instruction File* are architecture questions adjacent to `brand-context.md` and deserve a real read on a future ops run. Filed to the backlog, along with the tag-container hygiene half of `/gtm-audit` (duplicate tags, orphaned tags, trigger coverage, unpublished drafts), which is the mechanical sibling of what shipped today.
