### 2026-08-17 — Skill Scout: **ADD** — the target account list finally has an owner (automated)

**Scope:** scout survey, focus discipline **PMM / sales / GTM** by rotation (last the focus 2026-08-12; email/analytics/ops 08-13, content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-14, paid/social 08-16). 12 sources evaluated — **1 adopted-new (+3 co-credit)**, 2 watch, 8 dismissed. Full rows in [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**This run is an ADD, and deliberately so.** The four preceding scout runs (08-13, 08-14 ×2, 08-16) were all enhancements. Under the alternation rule the roster is meant to grow rather than only be edited, so the run opened by looking for a genuine missing role instead of a better version of an existing one.

**Shipped:** a new discipline `abm/` and a new skill `abm-ops`, holding one new agent — [`abm/abm-account-based-strategist.md`](../abm/abm-account-based-strategist.md) (**Account-Based Marketing Strategist**), dual-located at `plugins/saas-marketing/skills/abm-ops/agents/`. Roster **69 → 70 agents**, **18 → 19 skills**, **16 → 17 disciplines**. *(Structural change noted per the scout process: a new top-level discipline directory was created.)*

**Why a new discipline rather than `growth-ops`.** ABM is acquisition against named accounts, and the `growth-ops` skill explicitly scopes itself to "the two revenue surfaces that sit outside the classic top-of-funnel" and "hands anything pre-signup back to the acquisition skills." Filing ABM there would have required rewriting that skill's stated boundary and its router — a second change, in a one-change-per-run job. A one-agent discipline is also an established pattern here (`partnerships`, `events`, `developer-marketing` each hold one).

**The gap, and why it was load-bearing rather than merely missing.** The repo *already behaved as though this agent existed*. It ships a [Monthly ABM loop](../loops/monthly-abm-loop.md) with a persistent account ledger and a [worked ABM campaign workflow](../examples/workflow-abm-campaign.md); `ops-legal-compliance` names "the ABM program" as one of the channels that must query the single cross-channel suppression record before contacting anyone. Grep-verified before writing: **`target account list`, `account tier`, `buyer enablement` and `mutual action plan` returned zero repo-wide.** `ABM` and `account-based` returned hits only in *consumers* of a list somebody else was assumed to have produced — `paid-media-social-ads-specialist` (ABM ad targeting, account penetration metric), `sales-outbound-strategist` (account-based sequences, Bombora/6sense/Demandbase named as tools), `events-field-marketing-strategist` (per-event target accounts). And the ABM loop routed **Step 1, account scoring and tier changes, to `pmm-competitive-intelligence`** — a competitor-research agent — and committee mapping to `analytics-customer-insights-researcher`, a persona researcher. Those are workarounds, not owners: the repo's recurring *control named, instrument absent* pattern, here at the level of an entire program.

**Demand evidence, deliberately checked before writing.** Four independent permissively-licensed sources ship ABM as a first-class role: `gtmagents/gtm-agents` (a whole `abm-orchestration` plugin — strategist, analyst, account planner, tiering, signal-intel), `LeadMagic/gtm-skills` (the three motions as three skills), `lpalokan/bmad-marketing-growth` (`abm-strategist` + `abm-program` + `account-dossier`), `modest-curator478/claude-skills` (`revops/abm-campaign-builder`). Independent arrival at the same role is why the gap was treated as real rather than as one author's org chart.

**Nine things are ours.** (a) **Capacity sets the list, ambition does not** — the size is derived (accounts a paired seller can genuinely work × sellers paired × per-tier touch density), and when ambition exceeds capacity the instruction is to **cut the list, never thin the touches**, because thinning buys the worst outcome available: full cost, no depth, no coverage. (b) **The account list is also a media asset with a documented floor** — LinkedIn requires a company list of **≥300 rows** matching **≥300 member accounts** to run in an active ad set, so a 15-account Tier 1 tier *cannot* be a standalone paid audience and a touch plan that assumed air cover across every tier has a silent hole in it; the executability check therefore runs **before** the orchestration contract is published, while every delivery mechanic is routed to the paid agents. No surveyed source connects the tier model to the platform floor. (c) **No named sales owner is the go/no-go answer, not a scheduling problem.** (d) **MQLs are not a weak ABM metric but a misleading one** — they understate a working program and flatter one buying cheap engagement from the wrong accounts. (e) **Two corroborating signal families and a declared freshness window before any high-effort play**, with an undated signal treated as no signal. (f) **ABM takes assist credit, never a second copy of a pipeline dollar another channel already claimed** — with the model itself routed to `paid-media-attribution-analyst` rather than re-derived, and the comparison set behind any win-rate or cycle-length delta **published alongside the result**. (g) The repo's standing anti-fabrication posture applied where it bites hardest: **account research is the highest-fabrication-risk work in the repo**, so firmographics, funding, headcount, tech stack, named contacts and "current initiatives" carry `[NEEDS INPUT: …]` rather than fluent invention. (h) **Judge a cycle on account progression, not closed revenue** — most B2B buying cycles outlast one review period. (i) An explicit **boundary rule (Rule 10)** naming the eight agents that own everything downstream, so the program publishes the account set and the contract and never executes the touches.

**Deliberately not adopted.** `coreyhaines31/marketingskills`' `abm-playbook.md` is a detailed *paid* ABM playbook (Meta identity-graph match rates, acceleration campaigns, frequency-capping exclusion audiences, channel splits) — real content, wrong owner; adopting it would have broken the boundary the agent is built around, so it is filed as a paid-media enhancement lead instead. Its ABM go/no-go thresholds (list size, deal size, budget floor) are unsourced round numbers and were not carried. `LeadMagic`'s gifting/Sendoso material was left alone as vendor-bound and `ops-legal-compliance` ground. The two repos whose whole subject is ABM (`ivangfalco/curated-abm-skills`, `vynx-hq/abm-skill`) **assert no licence and were left unread**.

**Sourcing.** Ideas-only, written from scratch; no text from any source reused. Credited in-file to gtm-agents (Apache-2.0) and the three MIT sources, all licences verified via the GitHub license API this run. The 1:1 / 1:few / 1:many taxonomy is attributed to ITSMA, now [Momentum ITSMA / MomentumABM](https://momentumabm.com/the-pioneers-of-account-based-marketing) ("We pioneered ABM at the turn of the millennium", read 2026-08-17); the ABM-type definitions circulating in secondary sources were **not** quoted, and the median spend-per-account figure that circulates with them was **not** asserted — no primary read supports it. The only platform fact asserted is quoted from [LinkedIn Marketing Solutions Help](https://www.linkedin.com/help/lms/answer/a423102), read 2026-08-17. **No coverage, penetration, win-rate, spend-per-account, or program benchmark figure appears anywhere in the agent** — the file tells the reader to baseline from their own first cycles.

**Discoverability (counts changed, so the full sweep ran).** README (tagline, install line, feature bullet, discipline table, skills table, and **both badges**), `AGENTS_INDEX.md` (new section + jump-to link + header counts), `llms.txt` (summary, agent-index line, skills list, CATALYST line), `CITATION.cff`, both manifests, the suite router (description, two prose counts, discipline table, quick-reference table and its TOTAL), the CATALYST orchestrator `SKILL.md`, both copies of `catalyst-strategy.md` (whose functional-category lists were re-summed to 70), `strategy/QUICKSTART.md`, `strategy/EXECUTIVE-BRIEF.md`, `integrations/README.md`, `loops/README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and the **live GitHub About description** (`gh repo edit`, 350/350 chars, now naming "ABM target-account lists"). Grep for `69` / `18 skill` / `16 discipl` outside `maintenance/` and the historical `CHANGELOG` entries returns nothing.

**Also wired (part of shipping the agent, not a second change):** `loops/monthly-abm-loop.md` Step 1 moved from `pmm-competitive-intelligence` to the new agent (with competitive intel retained for account news), Step 6 gained it as the owner of the scoreboard and credit rule, and the loop's owner line now names it. The example workflow names no agents, so it needed no change.

**Verified:** lint **70/70**; the two copies of the new agent are diff-identical; **19** skill directories each carry a `SKILL.md`; both manifests parse; **436 internal links checked, 0 broken**; the two new external URLs return content (LinkedIn help page and the Momentum page were both read this run); every dual-located agent pair is byte-identical except the one known, intended relative-link difference in `catalyst-strategy.md`; the catalyst functional-category counts re-sum to 70.

**Filed for future runs:** the `coreyhaines31` paid-ABM playbook as an enhancement lead for `paid-media-social-ads-specialist`; the `gtm-agents` ABM command architecture as an architecture-run watch. The `modest-curator478` 08-13 watch is **half-closed** — its `revops/` ABM skill is worked; its `leadership/` tree still feeds the open grade-the-function SCOPE QUESTION.

### 2026-08-18 — Skill Scout: **ENHANCE** — the audit finally has a standard to grade against (automated)

**Scope:** scout survey, focus discipline **email / analytics / ops** by rotation — last the focus on 2026-08-13, the oldest of the four (content/SEO-AEO-GEO 08-14 and again 08-18 on the maintenance track, paid/social 08-16, PMM/sales/GTM 08-17). 12 sources evaluated — **1 enhanced-ours (+3 co-credit)**, 1 watch, 4 dismissed, 1 declined on licensing. Full rows in [scout-ledger.md](scout-ledger.md).

**This run is an ENHANCE, and deliberately so.** The 08-17 run was an ADD (`abm-account-based-strategist`, a new discipline and skill). Under the alternation rule the roster is not meant to grow every run, so this one went looking for the best-prepared enhancement instead — and found it already queued: the 2026-08-05 backlog item asking **who owns the instrumentation contract**, which had been blocked for thirteen days on a scope question.

**Shipped:** both dual-located copies of [`analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md`](../analytics/analytics-marketing-ops-architect.md) — a new **Critical Rule 10**, a **"The Instrumentation Contract: Deciding What an Event Means Before Anyone Counts It"** section, a **Marketing Instrumentation Contract** deliverable, and an **Instrumentation Contract Coverage** success metric. No count changed (70 agents, 19 skills, 17 disciplines), so no badge, manifest, `CITATION.cff`, or About-description sweep was owed.

**The scope question, answered.** The backlog flagged the prior question as a maintainer-shaped call: is the instrumentation contract a section on the Ops Architect or something larger? Answered **marketing slice, as a section**, and the boundary is stated in-file rather than implied. In scope: campaign response, form and lead capture, content and conversion events, and lifecycle-stage transitions — the events crossing the site, the ad platforms, the MAP and the CRM. Out: the in-product event taxonomy behind activation, TTV and the self-serve funnel, which `growth-plg-activation-strategist` already owns as a **Product Event Taxonomy & Activation Tracking Plan** deliverable in more depth than a marketing contract should attempt; and pipeline/warehouse/container implementation, declined for the same reason the mechanical tag audit was on 2026-08-02.

**The gap, grep-verified before writing.** `event schema`, `event version` and `schema version` returned **zero** repo-wide outside `maintenance/`. `tracking plan` resolved to exactly four real places, none of them an owner: one handoff checklist row, one `phase-3-build` ad-UTM bullet, one example-workflow deliverable line, and the PLG agent's *product* taxonomy. `event taxonomy` hit only the PLG agent and `devmkt-developer-audience-strategist`'s funnel spec. So the repo had a downstream audit layer that was real and well-developed — seven GA4 checks (07-27), the consent layer (08-01), tag integrity (08-02), the field layer (07-30) — and no upstream standard for any of it to grade against. This is the recurring *control named, instrument absent* pattern one level up: the instrument exists and the **standard** is absent.

**Six things are ours.** (a) The organising argument, drawn from the file's own contents: **check 1 asks whether the *right* events are key events, check 2 whether a custom definition was *worth* a scarce slot, check 6 whether the UTM vocabulary is the *right* one — right by whose decision?** Without a written contract the audit substitutes the auditor's judgement, and two competent people produce two defensible-but-different findings. An audit without a contract is a code review with no spec. (b) **Write it backward from decisions, never forward from the interface** — the inventory-of-what-is-clickable is the wrong artifact; every event names the report it feeds, and the **two gap lists** (decisions with no supporting event; events no decision reads) ship with the contract as the only honest completeness statement. The cost argument is made in this file's own currency: check 2's custom-definition cap is scarce and mostly not reclaimable without losing history, so *instrument it now in case we want it later* is reasonable against a warehouse and bad against a registration quota. (c) The **plausible-numbers-rather-than-errors signature**, named across three sections of one page for the first time: a consent gap leaves no error and a smaller correct-looking number, a duplicated tag (check 7) leaves no error and a larger one, a casing split leaves no error and two correct-looking fragments. (d) **Grading the enumeration** — allowed-value lists marked complete / incomplete / **sampled**, so a list derived by observing production is never presented as closed (*unknown never rounds to complete*, the standing rule applied to a new object). (e) **One conversion, four counts, one named authority per fact** — GA4's key event, the ad platform's conversion, the MAP's form submission and the CRM's lead are four different quantities produced by different windows, dedup rules, bot filtering, consent gating and arrival timing, not four estimates of one; the authority is named **per fact, not per tool**, and *crediting* is routed to `paid-media-attribution-analyst` so the contract governs counting only. (f) **You may change what an event means, or keep its name — not both**, with breaking changes required to ship as a new name or explicit version and to land in the **series-break register** `analytics-performance-analyst` maintains. That closes a real loop: the register's first three rows are instrumentation changes it names this agent to confirm, and this change log is now their upstream author, which is what lets the register be complete rather than remembered.

**Adopted (ideas only, written from scratch).** Four independent permissively-licensed sources ship the tracking plan as a first-class authored artifact, which is why the gap was treated as real rather than as one author's preference: [krillinai/growth-skills](https://github.com/krillinai/growth-skills) (an authoritative source per fact; the backward-compatible-vs-breaking split), [haiqigeng/ga4-tracking-plan](https://github.com/haiqigeng/ga4-tracking-plan) (business journeys and analysis decisions rather than click inventories; official-first with a documented gap before any custom construct; finite value domains graded rather than assumed exhaustive; drift reported without silently mutating the plan), [samikshapattanaik-cmyk/ga4-analytics-data-quality-suite](https://github.com/samikshapattanaik-cmyk/ga4-analytics-data-quality-suite) (drift as three distinct shapes — absent, wrong-typed, name/casing fragmentation), and [skohltfarber/google-analytics-growth-codex-skill](https://github.com/skohltfarber/google-analytics-growth-codex-skill) (the plan-then-verify ordering). All four licences verified MIT via the GitHub licence API this run. No text from any source was reused.

**Deliberately not adopted.** `haiqigeng`'s architecture — Playwright journey discovery, XLSX round-trip delivery, canonical JSON schemas, plan-diff and drift scripts — is the executable direction this repo has declined repeatedly; only its ideas were taken. `samikshapattanaik`'s severity convention and its "(tier inferred from notes — verify)" flag are our own four-state / unknown-never-rounds-to-pass discipline arrived at independently, so they were noted rather than credited as adoptions. [Chase-Zzx/user-behavior-analytics-skill](https://github.com/Chase-Zzx/user-behavior-analytics-skill) was directly on-thesis and would have been a fifth independent arrival, but **asserts no licence** (GitHub licence API 404, 2026-08-18) and was left unread on licensing, not on merit. `gabros20/data-skill` is analytics-engineering ground and out of scope.

**No fabricated metrics.** No coverage, drift-rate, reconciliation-gap, or benchmark figure appears anywhere in the section — the contract's own two gap lists are the baseline, measured in the reader's stack. The two GA4 behaviours asserted are quoted verbatim from primary sources read 2026-08-18: [Recommended events](https://support.google.com/analytics/answer/9267735) ("You should send recommended events with their prescribed parameters to get the most details in your reports and to benefit from future features and integrations as they become available") and [Event naming rules](https://support.google.com/analytics/answer/13316687) ("Event names are case sensitive. For example, `my_event` and `My_Event` are distinct events"). The custom-definition quota is referenced by pointing at check 2 rather than re-asserted, so the file carries one citation for it, not two.

**Discoverability (no count change, so the naming surfaces only).** The agent's frontmatter `description:` now names the contract and its searchable synonyms (tracking plan, event taxonomy, UTM taxonomy), with the matching row updated in `AGENTS_INDEX.md` and in the `marketing-analytics` skill's agent table; the skill's `description:` frontmatter gained the phrases a marketer would actually type (`tracking plan`, `event taxonomy`, `UTM taxonomy`, `GA4 audit`, `consent mode`, `instrumentation contract`); and the README trigger-phrase table and `llms.txt` skill line both gained the same. Grep confirms the three copies of the old description string are all updated.

**Verified:** lint 2/2; the two copies are diff-identical; both new external URLs return HTTP 200 and were read this run; no new internal links introduced, so the repo's link state is unchanged.

**Filed for future runs:** [sidchaudhary/gtm-skills](https://github.com/sidchaudhary/gtm-skills) on `watch` — 78 GTM skills with a deep `references/` tree whose `data-input-integrity`, `audit-findings-discipline` and `bayesian-testing` files are adjacent to disciplines we hold, but zero stars and one day old, so it fails the survived-contact test and must not be adopted on sight.
